# eva.md v1 design

This is the design of eva.md v1. Capture, resume and wiki updates are built, with system pages, standing rules, knowledge pages, pointers and prompt recall, and so are support for a wiki on a self-hosted git server, the release flow, and the install, upgrade and uninstall of a release. They are checked offline; no live update has run yet, and the live checks still owed are listed in [verification.md](verification.md). Parts marked "planned" are designed but not built yet. Host behaviour not yet observed is listed under [Open host questions](#open-host-questions). The agreed decisions behind it are in [decisions.md](decisions.md), and what is checked is in [verification.md](verification.md).

**Names:** the product is eva.md. The technical name is `eva-md` (binary, plugin, state folders). The wiki repo is `eva-wiki`.

---

## Summary

eva.md gives Claude Code and Codex memory that works without your help. When you open a new chat, run /clear, or the context compacts, and you ask "where did we stop?", the agent carries on as if it were the same chat.

How v1 does it:

- **Capture.** Hooks on both hosts save every prompt, every final reply, one line for each file change, command, read, search, web fetch and MCP call (by what it acted on, never its output), and the agent's plan updates. Secrets are redacted before anything is written. Entries go to a local inbox on each machine that is never committed.
- **Resume.** At the start of every chat, after /clear and after compaction, code (not a model) builds a short brief. It contains the branch's Now section, the last exchanges word for word, the open plan and the live git state, marked as data. So "where did we stop?" works even if no model has run yet.
- **Background update.** After a chat, a detached job runs the same host and model as the chat, headless. It rewrites the branch's Now section, appends dated Timeline lines, keeps the standing rules the user stated and promotes lasting facts, decisions, procedures and constraints into knowledge pages. The model returns strict JSON. Code then checks it before anything is written: every point cites its source entry, dates and speakers come from the entries, and no open item disappears without a reason.
- **System pages.** An agent digests documentation and code into one page per system through `eva-md system write`, with a map. Briefs and prompts that name a system point at its page.
- **Knowledge and recall.** A knowledge page holds one lasting statement with its sources and history. A prompt that strongly matches one gets the statement itself, fenced as reference notes, at most 3 a prompt; a new chat's brief lists the project's constraints. The brief reads one local index file, and recall reads it too, with the small repos index when it needs to know which projects share a system page; neither runs a model.
- **Storage.** Pages are written through okf, a Go library compiled in at a pinned version (v0.8.0), behind one adapter. The wiki is committed once a day with one signing attempt and pushed right after.

v1 is one Go binary plus okf, two static plugins with two skills and one MCP server, and about ten hook events. `eva-md search` reads a rebuildable index of the inbox and scans what the index lacks and the wiki's thread, system and knowledge pages, and the binary's read-only MCP mode offers that search, `eva-md show` and a project status as tools. Knowledge pages, the recall skill and prompt recall were built at the user's request rather than after two weeks of use (see "Changes to decisions").

Facts about okf and the hosts that shaped the design:
- okf already lets the caller set the author.
- okf rewrites a page's timestamp on every save.
- okf doesn't fsync the folder after writing.
- okf's index is unsorted and its log is newest-first in UTC, so eva-md writes both files itself.
- The author string must have no spaces.
- Claude plugin versions are strings.
- Codex's shell tool is called `Bash`.
- Codex's headless run cannot turn every tool off, so eva-md rejects any run that used a tool.

---

## How it works

```
Claude Code (CLI, desktop Code tab)            Codex (CLI, ChatGPT app Codex mode)
 plugin eva-md (own local marketplace)           plugin eva-md (own local marketplace)
   hooks/hooks.json (exec form)                    hooks/hooks.json (shell string, || echo '{}')
          \____ ~/.local/share/eva-md/bin/eva-md hook --host <h> ____/
                one Go binary, short-lived, always exits 0, no model, no network
     |                        |                                   |
  capture                 brief (built by code)            spawn detached (setsid)
  redact -> append JSONL  Now + last exchanges             eva-md update  (SessionEnd, PreCompact,
  ~/.local/state/eva-md/  + open plan + live git             60 KB backlog, 30-min gap, catch-up)
   inbox/<project>/       fenced, <= 8,000 (Codex 6,000)   eva-md maintain (first event of the day:
   <host>-<session>.jsonl -> additionalContext               commit + push, lint, code status, index; --push-only retries)
   (0600, never committed)                                        |
                                                                  v
          eva-md update: lock -> numbered entries -> same host + model, headless,
          no tools, JSON schema (claude -p ... | codex exec ...) -> validate in code
                                                                  |
                         okf adapter (the only code that calls okf):
                         lock, no-clobber create, compare-and-swap, skip unchanged,
                         actor check, body pre-check, folder fsync, own log + index
                                                                  v
      <wiki folder chosen at setup> (a private repo, for example on GitHub or a self-hosted GitLab)
        projects/<p>/now.md (overview, built by code)  projects/<p>/threads/<branch>.md (Now + Timeline)
        projects/<p>/rules.md, general/rules.md (standing rules)   systems/<slug>.md + systems/map.md
        knowledge/k<N>.md + knowledge/map.md (lasting facts, decisions, procedures, constraints)
        general/   index.md files (sorted, no timestamps)   log.md (append-only, union merge)
        .eva-md/schema.toml, .eva-md/names.tsv, .eva-md/repos.tsv, .eva-md/knowledge.tsv (the recall index)
                                                                  |
          daily: git add <paths> -> leave out inbox/tmp files and secret hits -> git commit
          (your signing, one approval) -> git push (retried later, never forced)
```

All of this is built: capture, the brief, eva-md update with its triggers and page writes, system pages, knowledge pages with prompt recall, and eva-md maintain with the daily commit and push.

Rules that hold it together:
- Hooks only append, read and spawn. The only model call is in a detached job, and code checks its output before anything is written.
- The resume path reads the inbox and git directly, so it never waits for the update.
- Nothing stays running. Every process starts from a host event and exits; the MCP server lives only as long as the chat that started it.
- The inbox is the word-for-word record and is stored only on this Mac; the background update sends each chat's new, redacted entries to that chat's own host CLI, and so to the model provider the chat already uses. The wiki is the curated view and is committed daily. Pending work is worked out from per-session watermarks, never queued.
- Wikis are kept apart by a per-machine config, a remote guard and an instance id stored in both the wiki and the config.

---

## Components

| Component | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| **eva-md binary** | One Go executable: CLI and hook handler, the updater, the daily job and system page writes, and the read-only MCP server (see "MCP server"). Installed at `~/.local/share/eva-md/bin/eva-md` with an atomic replace. Non-standard dependencies: `github.com/okf-memory/okf-agent-memory/pkg/okf` (pinned v0.8.0) and a TOML parser. Only `internal/okfadapter` imports okf. Init, validate, the doctor and the brief's page reads go through it; capture never does. |
| **Static plugins** (one per host) | Connect the hook events to the binary through an absolute path generated once per machine. The plugin folders are byte-identical across binary-only releases. Generated from embedded templates into two separate local marketplace folders, each with the digest and recall skills (see Packaging). |
| **Hook dispatcher** | Reads the payload and applies the guards (see Capture). Resolves the project from the payload's `cwd`, then redacts, appends, builds briefs, adds system pointers, recalled knowledge and the reminder to prompts, writes heartbeats, and spawns the update job and maintain detached. Internal deadlines: 1.5 s for SessionStart and for PostToolUse and PostToolUseFailure, which the plugins mark async, so Claude Code doesn't wait for them and a burst of parallel tool calls waiting its turn for the session lock is no failure (a host that did wait would wait longer on a contended lock, where it used to lose the line); 300 ms for every other event. Never exits 2. Prints nothing except a brief, a prompt's system pointers, recalled knowledge or memory reminder, a notice (including the chat-start line for a config that can't be read), an off-the-record confirmation, or `{}` on Codex Stop. |
| **Identity resolver** | Hardened git reads (`-c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null --no-optional-locks`, with prompts, optional locks and lazy fetches off through `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` and `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, and git's pathspec settings, such as `GIT_GLOB_PATHSPECS`, left out, since the code reads name paths with `--literal-pathspecs`, which git refuses beside them; 800 ms timeout). Finds the worktree root, the branch (from its full ref name, so a tag of the same name changes nothing) and the normalized origin as configured in `remote.origin.url`, without `insteadOf` rewrites: credentials stripped, host lowercased, `.git` removed. A repo without a remote is identified by its folder. Anything outside a repo goes to general. Results are cached per worktree in `~/.local/state/eva-md/cache/identity/`, one 0600 entry per worktree (or per folder outside a repo). A hook reuses an entry, with no git process, only while a fresh look at what git reads is unchanged: the folders' identity and owner, the `.git` file, HEAD, every config file git reads (system, global, the repo's, `config.worktree` and their includes), packed-refs and the branch's loose ref. The project key also depends on the redaction rules (see "The inbox"), which an upgrade can change while git's files stay the same, so each hit derives the key again from the entry's remote or folder, and an entry whose key differs resolves live and is replaced. Anything unusual, such as a symlinked path, `core.worktree` or another ref named HEAD, resolves live. |
| **Redactor** | Versioned rules (`redact.Version` 5). The text removed is the union of what the baseline rules (the frozen original rules in `internal/redact/baseline`) and the current rules remove, so no rule change can keep a secret the baseline rules remove. Each current rule runs only when cheap hints find what a match needs, such as a `pass` key followed by `=` or `:`, or `mysql` or `curl`, so a text without them costs a few substring scans; a command rule reads each command once, word by word, so its cost grows with the text however many command names a line holds. Identity fields (project keys, branch names) get format-only checks without the entropy rule. Used at capture, on model output and on the staged diff before each commit. That scan (`wikigit.SecretLines`) judges each line alone and flags it when redaction would replace anything but identifier-shaped words, all-caps parts such as issue keys and words ending in digits counting as words; system page writes, `eva-md show` and search snippets judge wiki lines the same way. Fails closed: an entry is stored without its text rather than unredacted. Logs keep counts only. |
| **Inbox store** | Append-only JSONL, one file per (project, host, session). Written under flock with O_APPEND, with fsync on user and assistant entries. A byte-offset watermark per file, marked "more" when the last update pass stopped inside a group, and with the file's size when the update found the rest too small to process yet. Per-session marker files: off the record, briefed, model, thread, and `prompts`, the per-prompt pointer state, kept apart from briefed because it changes on every prompt and is written in place (see "Per prompt"). |
| **Catch-up reader** | Reads a known transcript forward from a saved cursor, one 1 MiB window per pass, saving the cursor after every row, so a pass that runs out of time resumes where it stopped. A longer row is scanned in bounded chunks, honours a record control and leaves a gap event. A row of 256 KiB or more records an attempt before it is decoded. A shorter row whose decoding uses up a pass is marked slow, so the next pass loads the stored entries before decoding it and can skip or bound it. Claude user rows count as prompts only when they carry a promptId and no host wrapper; meta rows, compaction summaries, sidechains and synthetic loader rows are skipped. Codex is best-effort, because its transcript format is not a stable interface. Entries are marked `origin=catchup` and deduplicated by turn id or a text hash. Recovered rows are the main thread's and carry no agent id. A Codex `turn_context` whose cwd differs from the registered folder marks the chat as elsewhere (the recovery state's `elsewhere`): recovery reads on but stores none of that turn's rows, still honouring an off-the-record control in them, in the same pass or a later one, and stores again from a `turn_context` back in the registered folder; a registration that starts a new baseline clears the mark. Recovery is blocked only while the chat is off the record: a registration keeps a block only when the off marker is set or the block's reason is `off_record`, and a block an older version saved for a turn in another folder is lifted at the next registration, which starts again at the transcript's end. A transcript shorter than the cursor, as one truncated or rewritten in place, starts again at its end when a hook next registers the chat (at most once per 10 seconds for an unchanged registration); until then a pass fails with `transcript replaced or truncated`, which the hook treats as caught up. |
| **Brief builder** | Picks the previous chat, builds the startup, compact, resume and refresh variants within per-host budgets, shows the standing rules, the project's constraints and the Now by priority, adds the opening request, earlier chats, other branches, tool activity, live git, freshness lines, system pointers and the footer, and computes the data fence. |
| **Updater** (built, with its triggers) | One job at a time under its own lock, draining each session's backlog in chunks, at most 40 model calls per host per day. Resolves the chat's model, builds the prompt from numbered entries, runs the host CLI headless with a JSON schema, validates the result, writes the knowledge, rules and thread pages and records every pass in a daily run log. |
| **Validator** | Deterministic checks before writing (`Validate` in `internal/update`): strict schema, refs, speaker tags, pasted text, sourced dates, item accounting, standing rules, knowledge, idempotency, out-of-order guard, locked pages, caps, escaping, and redaction again. |
| **Renderer + okf adapter** | The renderer (`internal/threadpage`, built and tested, used by the update job) turns the old page plus validated operations into the new page. The Now section is rewritten and the Timeline is only appended to. The adapter is the only code that calls okf (next section). |
| **Maintain / committer** | `eva-md maintain` builds the files listing the knowledge pages again when they are out of date, then runs the daily commit and push with all guards (`internal/wikigit`, which also holds the hardened git runner init uses), then the wiki lint (`internal/lint`), the code status of the system pages (`internal/coderef`, see "Code a system page cites"), the removal of old attempt markers and of the transcript-recovery records not saved for 30 days or past a host's newest 2,048 (`internal/catchup`), and last the search index (see "Search index"); with no wiki set up it keeps the search index and removes those markers and records. The day's sweep of idle backlogs runs in the day's first catch-up job instead, so it never holds back the commit (see "Triggers"). |
| **System pages** | `internal/systempage` checks and writes system pages, the system map, the names index and the repos index (see "System page"); the hook's prompt pointers read the names index, and prompt recall the repos index. |
| **Knowledge pages** | `internal/knowledgepage` reads, renders and saves knowledge pages, and builds what lists them: the knowledge map, the folder index, the root index's line and the recall index, `.eva-md/knowledge.tsv` (see "Knowledge page"). The update writes the pages; the prompt hook, the brief, the update's input and `eva_status` read the recall index, never the pages. |
| **Code refs** | `internal/coderef` measures how far the code a system page cites has moved, with bounded, read-only git calls in a local clone, and keeps what a pass measured in `<state>/code-status.json`. The identity cache doubles as its registry of clones, since it holds an entry for every worktree a chat ran in (see "Code a system page cites"). |
| **Search index** | `internal/searchindex`: a rebuildable cache of the inbox's lines that `eva-md search` reads beside its scan, kept by the daily maintain and `eva-md index` (see "Search index"). Hooks never read or write it. |
| **init / install / uninstall / doctor** | Set up the wiki and the per-machine config. Install only through the hosts' plugin CLIs, verify, and roll back only this run's steps; record what install made and link the binary on PATH where that is safe. The doctor reports PASS, FAIL or UNKNOWN. |
| **Releases and upgrade** | `internal/release` finds the latest release, downloads an asset and checks it against `SHA256SUMS`, and orders versions; `eva-md upgrade` uses it, and `scripts/install.sh` and `scripts/uninstall.sh` find, download and check a release the same way in the shell (see "The install scripts and upgrade"). |

**Git's environment.** Every git eva-md runs starts from one environment (`identity.GitEnviron`): the identity and code reads, the brief's live git, the update's commit subjects, the wiki's runner, which the daily commit, init and setup use, and setup's read of your signing settings, so its signing line says what the daily commit will do; gh and glab get theirs from it in setup. It is this process's, without the variables that point git at another repository (those git itself clears before it runs a command in another repository, such as `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and `GIT_SHALLOW_FILE`, and `GIT_NAMESPACE`), carry config (`GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, which `git -c` hands down, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` with its keys and values, and `GIT_CONFIG`), change how git reads a pathspec (`GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS`, `GIT_GLOB_PATHSPECS`, `GIT_NOGLOB_PATHSPECS` and `GIT_ICASE_PATHSPECS`) or write a trace of what git does (every `GIT_TRACE*` and `GIT_CURL_VERBOSE`), and with prompts off (`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`) and git's messages in English (`LC_ALL=C`): eva-md reads them to sort a failure, and a git built with translations, such as Homebrew's, would word them in your locale's language. git's config files (`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`) and what signs and signs in (`GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, `GIT_ASKPASS`, the SSH agent) pass. The read-only calls add `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` and `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`.

**Command output.** What eva-md keeps of a command's output is capped, and the cap holds however much the command prints. A git command of the wiki's runner fails past 1 MiB, but for the daily commit's list of staged files and uninstall's count of changed files, which may take 64 MiB, a few hundred thousand files, and setup's and init's check for uncommitted changes, which only notes whether git listed any, so a list of any length counts as changes; what git prints on stderr, read only to sort a failure, is cut at 1 MiB. The brief keeps the first 64 KiB of each live git call. A host's plugin inventory fails past 2 MiB, and what the host prints on stderr is cut there. The doctor takes a host command, such as `codex features list`, that prints more than 8 MiB as unavailable, and setup fails a gh or glab read that prints more than 1 MiB.

---

## The okf adapter

okf (okf-memory/okf-agent-memory, MIT) owns the page format: parsing, serializing, safe single-file writes and validation. When a wiki is set up, it writes the new wiki's pages, and eva-md writes the root index and the log. `eva-md search` makes no okf call: it reads the inbox and the thread, system and knowledge pages itself (see the Search row below). One Go file in eva-md, `internal/okfadapter`, is the only code that imports `pkg/okf`. It adds the protections okf lacks today. Each protection is removed once an okf release covers it.

### okf calls per operation

| Operation | Exact calls (okf v0.8.0) and eva-md steps |
| --- | --- |
| **Open the wiki** | `filepath.EvalSymlinks(wiki)`. Check that `<root>/index.md` is a regular file. Without a root index.md, okf silently treats `knowledge/` as the wiki (bundle.go:160-172), so this check is required. Opening reads no pages, so the brief reads only the pages it needs and its cost doesn't grow with the wiki. **Validate** is the only call that loads the whole wiki: `okf.LoadBundle(root)`, then a check that `b.RootPath == root`. |
| **Read a page** | `os.ReadFile`, then `okf.ParseConcept(relPath, content)`. Keep a SHA-256 of the raw bytes for compare-and-swap. |
| **Create a thread or page** | Take the wiki lock. `okf.ValidateConceptID(id)`. `os.Lstat` must report the file missing, otherwise refuse. Check `okf.IsValidActor(actor)`. Remove a leftover okf lock file (see the protections below). Then `okf.SaveConcept(root, c, okf.SaveOptions{IsNew: true, AutoLog: false, AutoIndex: false, Actor: actor})`. fsync the folder. Regenerate the folder's index.md. |
| **Update a thread** | Take the wiki lock. Re-read the file and compare its hash with the one read before rendering. If it differs, re-apply the operations by item id (at most 2 retries). Render the body with escaping. Copy the parsed Concept, keeping `Extra`, `Verified` and `Sources`. If the new page equals the file on disk ignoring the `generated` line, skip the save. Otherwise remove a leftover okf lock file and call `okf.SaveConcept(..., SaveOptions{IsNew: false, AutoLog: false, AutoIndex: false, Actor: actor})`. fsync the folder. Only then advance the watermark. |
| **Project now.md, README.md** | Same as update, with actor `eva-md/<version>`. |
| **Writes that must land together** | `Wiki.Locked` runs several writes under one hold of the wiki lock, such as a system page with the map and the folder index, a pass's knowledge pages with the knowledge map, the folder index, the recall index and the root index's line, a project's first `now.md` with its thread page, or the root index's lines. |
| **index.md files** | eva-md writes them itself (see wiki layout), using okf's exact line format and its own atomic write plus folder fsync, under the wiki lock. `UpdateParentIndex` is not called. |
| **log.md** | eva-md writes it itself, with its own atomic write, under the wiki lock. Init writes the first `## YYYY-MM-DD` local-date group with its creation line. After that, each `Create` or `Update` that saves a page adds one line in okf's own words, ``* **Creation**: Documented concept `<path>` (<title>).`` or ``* **Update**: Updated concept `<path>`.``, to the local date's group at the end of the file, oldest first, the same line once a day. A log that is missing, a link or over 16 MiB gets no line, and the page write stands. okf's `SaveConcept` refuses log.md because it is a reserved name. |
| **Init** | eva-md's own checks. Write log.md and eva-md's own root index.md first, then remove a leftover okf lock file and call `okf.InitBundle(root)`, which skips both because they exist, so okf's index, which names okf, is never written. Then create README.md, general/now.md and general/threads/default.md as above, write the full root index.md, general/index.md, general/threads/index.md, `.gitattributes` and `.gitignore`, and last `.eva-md/schema.toml`. |
| **Validate** | Open the wiki as above, then `okf.Validate(b, okf.ValidateOptions{Drift: true, Strict: false})`. `eva-md validate` prints the counts and fails on any error, orphan or broken link, and on an invalid-actor or parent-index warning. The doctor's wiki validation row fails on errors, broken links and orphans. |
| **Search** | Not called: `eva-md search` reads its own rebuildable index of the inbox (see "Search index") and scans what the index lacks and the thread, system and knowledge pages itself, one file at a time, because `SearchAdvanced` reloads and re-tokenizes the whole wiki for every query. Never `SearchLayered`: it falls back to the working directory, `~/.okf`, `/etc/okf` and `OKF_*` environment variables (scope.go:182-237). |
| **Never called** | `AppendLogEntry`, `RelateConcepts` (it always logs, adds a level-1 section and bumps the timestamp), `UpdateParentIndex`, `UpdateConcept` (it loads the whole wiki to change one page), `WithBundleLock` (a `SaveConcept` inside it would wait for its own lock), `SearchLayered`, `Bootstrap`, `CreateToolSymlinks`, `RunMCPServer`, and anything outside `pkg/okf`. |

### Protections eva-md adds

| Gap in okf (v0.8.0) | eva-md protection | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Create silently overwrites: `IsNew` never refuses a file already at the path. It only changes the log wording, keeps a non-human save from carrying over that file's human verifications, and skips the compare-and-swap | Lstat under the wiki lock, refuse if the file exists | mutate.go:459-553, provenance.go:20-40 |
| Its lock is a file, `.okf-write.lock`, in the wiki's root, which `SaveConcept` and `InitBundle` hold for one call. It isn't reentrant, so it can't hold several writes together, and a save killed midway leaves it behind: okf then makes each save wait up to 10 seconds for it and refuses the save, until the file is 30 seconds old, and from then on tries to remove it itself, with no pause and no end when it can't, as in a wiki folder it can't write | One wiki lock (flock on `~/.local/state/eva-md/locks/wiki.lock`) around every write, which the kernel lets go of when a process dies. Under it, the adapter removes a leftover okf lock file before each okf call that takes one, as it does before Init, under init's lock (`<locks>/init.lock`). A leftover it can't remove fails the save at once with that error, in eva-md's words and keeping the errno, and okf isn't called. Setup and init remove a leftover one the same way, under the wiki lock, before they look for changes not yet committed: it is never yours, git doesn't ignore it, and the commit setup advises for such changes would take it. One they can't remove, or a wiki lock that stays busy, refuses the wiki with that reason, never with that advice | lock.go:13-21, 62-119 |
| Compare-and-swap only for a page `LoadBundle` loaded, which reads the whole wiki | Hash check under the lock, re-apply by id | mutate.go:498-506, bundle.go:403-406 |
| Every save rewrites `generated.at` | Skip the save when nothing but `generated` would change | mutate.go:508-511 |
| Actor not validated on write | `IsValidActor` before every save, with `<host>/unknown` as the fallback | types.go:19, validator.go:179-181 |
| No folder fsync after rename | fsync the parent folder before advancing any watermark | mutate.go:51-61 |
| A killed save leaves `.tmp-okf-*` files | `.gitignore`'s `.tmp-*` line, and the commit refuses them | mutate.go:38-45 |
| A save that fails on the file system names its `.tmp-okf-*` file, or its lock file, in the error | The adapter says the step that failed in its own words, keeping the errno; a lock file okf waited for in vain is a busy wiki lock | mutate.go:38-63, lock.go:80-91 |
| Body check rejects `---` blocks holding `status:` and similar lines | Escape model text before saving. If your own text trips the check, mark the page damaged | mutate.go:423-442 |
| Log is newest-first, dated in UTC | eva-md owns log.md | mutate.go:95-147 |
| Index is append-only and unsorted, never pruned | eva-md generates sorted index files in okf's format | mutate.go:304-319 |
| Missing root index makes LoadBundle switch to knowledge/ | Assert root index.md exists and RootPath matches. knowledge/ is created only by the first knowledge page write, in a wiki whose root index init wrote | bundle.go:160-172 |
| No supersede helper | The renderer writes dated strikethrough lines in the Timeline, and a knowledge page's History | no supersede helper in pkg/okf; its only match for supersed, validator.go:203, is a "superseded verification" finding |

okf already supports recording the real author through `SaveOptions.Actor` (mutate.go:489-511), so eva-md does not need to add that protection.

Side effects to accept:
- Pages are written with mode 0644 whatever the umask (mutate.go:58). The wiki is a private repo, and the inbox stays 0600.
- `SaveConcept` normalises leading and trailing blank lines in the body.

### Gaps covered in eva-md (watch okf for these)

eva-md opens no issues or pull requests on okf. Each gap below is covered on top of okf in eva-md's adapter. At every okf review, check whether a new release covers a gap. If it does, switch to okf's version and delete eva-md's.

1. No-clobber create, and compare-and-swap for a page read on its own.
2. A bundle-level write lock that can hold several writes together and that a killed process can't leave behind.
3. A folder fsync after writes, and honouring the umask or accepting a file mode.
4. Actor validation in `SaveConcept`.
5. Skipping unchanged saves, and a clock the caller can set.
6. Log options: an append mode and a time zone setting, and `RelateConcepts` respecting `AutoLog`.
7. A sorted, pruned index rebuild with folder links.
8. A `LoadBundle` mode with no knowledge/ redirect, or a flag saying the redirect happened.
9. A narrower body check, exported so callers can check text before saving.
10. A layered search that ignores the working folder and environment, plus matched terms and snippets in search results.
11. Round-tripping flow mappings in unknown frontmatter keys.
12. Escaping `[`, `]` and `)` in index titles.
13. A parser fix so `- s:abc` doesn't split at a colon with no space after it.

### Pin and upgrade policy

- **Pin.** `require github.com/okf-memory/okf-agent-memory v0.8.0`, go.sum committed, `go 1.26.0` or later in go.mod, builds with `-mod=readonly`. v0.7.1 is the minimum version: atomic writes arrived in v0.4.1, `SaveOptions` in v0.4.4, the verification guard in v0.6.0, and v0.7.1 stopped dropping unknown keys in sources. `golang.org/x/crypto` is in okf's module graph but is not compiled in, because only `pkg/vault` uses it. In v0.7.2, `pkg/okf` resolves a relative bundle root to an absolute path before following symlinks; eva-md always passes absolute, resolved roots. v0.8.0 added a lock file in the wiki's root for each save, which the adapter clears after a killed save (see the protections above), a compare-and-swap for pages `LoadBundle` loaded, and an optional `scopes` list in the root index's frontmatter that `LoadBundle` refuses when it is empty or names an unknown scope; eva-md writes no frontmatter in the root index and reads no scopes. Its parser and serializer are unchanged, so pages are saved byte for byte as before. The okf line references in this section are to v0.8.0.
- **Record.** eva-md records no okf version in a wiki, and names okf nowhere you see it but each release's `LICENSES.txt`, which carries okf's license as that license asks (see "Changes to decisions", 13 and 15). `.eva-md/schema.toml`'s `schema_version` is eva-md's own wiki format, whose pages are okf spec 0.2 pages, and `eva-md version` prints the binary's version and that schema. The binary's own version is its release tag, or for a source build the day, time and hash of its commit (see `eva-md version` in "Command-line surface").
- **Regular review.** okf is reviewed weekly. Each review checks new releases for security fixes and against the list of gaps covered in eva-md.
- **When to upgrade.** Upgrade for a security fix in path, ID or parser handling, or for a release that covers a gap eva-md works around, so its version can be deleted. Don't upgrade just because a release exists.
- **How.** Work on a branch named `okf-vX.Y.Z`:
  1. Read `docs/releases/vX.Y.Z.md` and `git diff old new -- pkg/okf/{mutate,parser,validator,bundle,types}.go`.
  2. Run apidiff on the identifiers the adapter calls.
  3. Run the adapter golden tests: Parse then Serialize must round-trip byte for byte on real eva.md pages. `SaveConcept` must produce the same bytes apart from `generated.at`. Validate must give the same result on a fixture wiki. A body-escaping corpus must still be accepted.
  4. Load and re-save every page of a copy of the live wiki, and expect `git diff --exit-code` to be clean.
  5. Merge only if all of these pass.
- **Treat every okf tag as possibly breaking, patch tags included.** v0.4.4 changed the `SaveConcept` signature in a patch release, and 7 of 20 releases changed behaviour in functions the adapter calls.
- **If an upgrade breaks things.** A source break is fixed inside the adapter only. If an upgrade changes page bytes or rejects content eva-md writes, stay on the current pin until eva-md's adapter handles the change. A temporary `replace` to a local fork is allowed only to apply a security fix; it changes the module's heading in `scripts/LICENSES.txt`, whose test gives the text to use. An upgrade never rewrites existing pages on its own.
- **If okf doesn't work out.** This applies if okf stops round-tripping eva.md pages byte for byte (the adapter golden tests check that it does), or if the adapter keeps growing past its protections.
  - eva-md then writes its own pages. That takes a few hundred lines: parsing and serializing its few page types, an atomic write with a folder fsync, and the lock.
  - okf stays as the format spec, and as the validator in the doctor and the tests.
  - The wiki format doesn't change, so your memory is untouched.
- **Not ai-memory as the core.** ai-memory was also evaluated as the core under eva-md, and not chosen. Under eva-md, its own capture and handoff would be switched off. What remains:
  - a page store behind a server that can't be reliably stopped
  - a second store in SQLite that the markdown can't rebuild
  - unsigned commits on every event, which can't be turned off
  - an ID-named layout, so your repo would hold only a copy

---

## Wiki layout and page formats

The wiki lives in a folder you choose during `eva-md setup` (there is no default), backed by a private repo. Each machine points at one wiki. The whole tree is one okf bundle. Setup creates README.md, the root index.md, log.md, `.eva-md/schema.toml`, `.gitattributes`, `.gitignore` and the general area with placeholder pages (general/now.md and general/threads/default.md). The placeholder thread page is rendered by the page model the background update uses (`internal/threadpage`), so the two can't drift. The `projects/` folder, its pages, the rules pages and real thread content are written by the background update, and so is the `knowledge/` folder, created by its first knowledge page. The `systems/` folder is written by `eva-md system write`.

```
README.md                       type: guide; what this is, how to read it, how to lock a page; links to general/now.md
index.md                        okf root index without frontmatter, written by setup and extended by code, no timestamps
log.md                          '## YYYY-MM-DD' local-date groups, oldest first: init's line, then a line for each page created or changed, once a day (owned by eva-md)
.eva-md/schema.toml             schema_version = 1, instance = "<machine>-<8 hex>", created   (hidden: okf ignores it)
.gitattributes                  log.md merge=union ; *.md text eol=lf
.gitignore                      *.jsonl  /inbox/  .eva-md/tmp/  .tmp-*  .DS_Store
projects/index.md               sub-index of project folders (no frontmatter)
projects/<slug>/index.md        lists now.md, rules.md once it exists, and threads/index.md
projects/<slug>/now.md          project overview, built by code
projects/<slug>/rules.md        the project's standing rules, linked from its now.md
projects/<slug>/threads/index.md
projects/<slug>/threads/<branch>.md   one thread per branch: Now on top, Timeline below
general/index.md, general/now.md, general/threads/index.md, general/threads/default.md
general/rules.md                rules for all projects, linked from general/now.md
systems/index.md                sub-index of system pages
systems/map.md                  the system map: one line per system page, built by code
systems/<slug>.md               one page per system, written by eva-md system write
.eva-md/names.tsv               the names index the prompt hook matches against
.eva-md/repos.tsv               the repos index: which projects share a system page, for recall
knowledge/index.md              sub-index of the knowledge pages and the map
knowledge/map.md                the knowledge map: one line per knowledge page, built by code
knowledge/k<N>.md               one lasting fact, decision, procedure or constraint, written by the update
.eva-md/knowledge.tsv           the recall index the prompt hook, the brief and the update read
```

The root index also lists `systems/index.md` after the first system write, and `knowledge/index.md` after the first knowledge page.

`knowledge/` is created by the first knowledge page, never by init. okf treats `knowledge/` as the wiki whenever the root index.md is missing, so opening the wiki checks that the root index.md exists (see "Open the wiki").

**Slugs.** The slug is the repo name, or owner-repo on a collision. Projects without a remote use `<folder>-<hash6>`. A project without a remote is found in its own slug folder, or in the folder with its key's hash (`identity.Project.HashedSlug`: the slug, `-` and the first 6 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the key), which the update takes when another project holds the plain name, and only when that folder's now page names no remote; another project's folder of the same name is never taken. The update, the brief and search look in both. The full normalized remote is stored in now.md frontmatter, and the map from identity to slug is rebuilt from it.

**Thread file names.** The branch name is turned into an okf-safe ID by one function (`threadpage.ThreadName`), which the brief, search and the update share:
- Any character other than a letter, a number, `_`, `-` or `.` becomes `-`, `/` included.
- A leading `.` or `-` gets a `_` prefix.
- The reserved names `index`, `log` and `AGENTS` (any case) get the suffix `-thread`.
- Control and bidi characters are removed. A branch with no name is `default`, and a name left empty is `thread`.
- A 6-character hash is added whenever any of these changed the name.

The ID `projects/<slug>/threads/<file>` has 4 parts, well under okf's limit of 8.

**Index files.**
- eva-md regenerates every sub-index in the folders a write touched, sorted by file name, in okf's exact line format: `* [Title](file.md) - Description`. The root index is written once by setup; each of `projects/`, `systems/` and `knowledge/` adds its line at the end the first time it appears, and the knowledge line goes again when the folder is gone.
- `[`, `]` and `)` in titles are escaped.
- The root index has no frontmatter, since okf's spec makes its `okf_version` line optional and a wiki names eva-md alone. It lists README.md plus a link to `general/index.md`, to `projects/index.md` once the update writes a project's first page, to `systems/index.md` after the first system write, and to `knowledge/index.md` after the first knowledge page. Sub-indexes have a `# <Folder>` header and no frontmatter.
- No timestamps. A line changes only when a title or description changes, and descriptions are built from fixed text, so normal updates don't touch the index files.

**Links.** okf counts a page as orphaned unless another page's body links to it or it links out; links in index.md files don't count. So:
- README.md links to `general/now.md`.
- Each now.md links to every one of its threads, including idle, merged and gone ones.
- No page ever links to an index.md or log.md, because okf counts those links as broken.

**Frontmatter rules.**
- okf writes keys in a fixed order: type, title, description, generated, verified, status, sources, then extra keys sorted alphabetically.
- eva-md's extra keys are flat: plain values or lists of plain values, never nested maps (okf turns nested maps into strings).
- `status` is always `stable`. eva-md's own lifecycle goes in `state`.
- `generated.by` must pass okf's actor check, so it can contain no spaces. It is `<host>/<model>`, for example `claude-code/claude-opus-5-5` or `codex/gpt-5.5-codex`. Any `/` or space inside the model name becomes `-`. It is `<host>/unknown` when the model is unknown, and `eva-md/<version>` for pages built by code.
- `generated.at` is always the UTC save time (okf sets it). The time of the content is `as_of`.
- `sources` items are mappings: `- resource: "s:3f2a91c0"`.

### Thread page

The thread page is the only page the model influences, and only through validated operations.

```
---
type: thread
title: "eva-md · main"
description: "Branch main of github.com/you/eva-md"
generated: { by: claude-code/claude-opus-5-5, at: "2026-01-15T09:14:02Z" }
status: stable
sources:
  - resource: "s:3f2a91c0"
  - resource: "s:91bd0e44"
as_of: "2026-01-15T10:13:55+01:00"
branch: "main"
head: "4e2bf4c1a9d3e8f07b6c5a2d1e9f8a7b6c5d4e3f"
locked: false
model: "claude-opus-5-5"
model_note: ""
parent: ""
project: "eva-md"
state: "active"
writer: "eva-md/2026.1.15+0912.9e4f609"
---
```

- `sources` holds the last 20 sessions.
- `model` is the raw model name.
- `model_note` is filled only for "fallback from X" or "default (chat model unknown)".
- `parent` is the thread this one started from, if any.
- `state` is `active`; idle, merged and gone, worked out from git, are planned.
- okf's serializer decides the exact quoting.
- `head` is the commit HEAD named when the page was written, which the brief's live git compares with HEAD ("HEAD moved N commits since the page was written", with the last 3 subjects). A thread page cites no code: its sources are sessions, the refs of its items are inbox entries the validator checks against each batch, which is what keeps a model from forging them, and an update reads nothing of a repo but the branch's last commit subjects and HEAD (see "Page context"); it cites code only on a knowledge page, at that HEAD, for paths a cited entry writes (see "Knowledge page"). Standing rules are the user's own words and cite none either.

Body (built in `internal/threadpage`, whose package comment has the exact format and a full sample):
- `# eva-md · main`
- `## Now`, between `<!-- eva:now:start -->` and `<!-- eva:now:end -->` markers:
  - summary, at most 600 characters
  - `### Last chat`: date, host/model, one line (at most 400 characters)
  - `### Done`: the last chat's results, up to 8 items, replaced each pass
  - `### Next`: up to 10 items
  - `### Open questions`: up to 10, including `Proposed (assistant): ...` items
  - `### Decisions in force`: up to 40, stated or accepted only
  - An item is one line: `- `, an optional label, the text, the tag in brackets, the refs, and a hidden comment with its id and the cited user prompt's time, for example `- Use JSONL for the inbox [stated] s:3f2a91c0#14 <!-- i:n3 t:2026-01-14T10:00:00+01:00 -->`. Empty sections are left out.
  - Labels: `Proposed (assistant): ` marks an Open question tagged suggested, `Unattributed: ` one tagged inferred, and `eva-md: <code>: ` a line eva-md wrote itself, which keeps its stored tag. No rule ever changes a speaker tag or shows user speech under an assistant label.
  - A brief removes the hidden comments and shows these sections by priority (see "The Now in a brief" under Recall and fencing).
- Caps never drop a decision. A decision added past 40 is kept under Open questions as `eva-md: decision over cap, kept: ...` with its tag. Next and Open questions past 10 move to the Timeline marked `(moved: cap)`: inferred and suggested items first, then the oldest, and never a stated item while an assistant-tagged one remains.
- `## Timeline`: `### YYYY-MM-DD` groups of lines reading `- <word> [<tag>]: <text> <refs>`, for example `- decision [stated]: Use JSONL for the inbox s:3f2a91c0#14`. Retired items keep their tag, refs and id comment: `done`, `answered: q → a`, `~~old~~ → new (superseded 2026-01-14)` and `~~item~~ (dropped: reason)`. A date out of order starts a new group at the end. Ids (`n<k>`) are never reused, and an item written by hand gets one at the next update.
- Timeline lines are never rewritten. Any other `##` section, such as your own `## Notes`, keeps its text byte for byte, placed after the Timeline.
- Free text goes through one escaper (see Validation 8), so no item can forge a heading, a rule, a comment, a fence, a link or a label. The renderer never writes a line that is exactly `---`.

**When a page is never written:**
- **Locked:** `locked: true`, `yes` or `on`, in any case.
- **Human-verified:** the page has a human `verified` entry, which would otherwise be marked superseded by every save (validator.go:200-205).
- **Damaged:** the eva markers are broken, or okf refuses the save because of text you wrote, for example horizontal rules around a `Status:` line. A notice explains the fix. eva-md never rewrites your sections.

### Project now.md

Built by code with no model, by the update job after each save. A project's first page write creates it first, holding the project's remote, so another project of the same name can't take the folder. This is decision 11's Now page, as an overview of the project's threads.
- **Frontmatter:** type: now, title, description (fixed text), generated `{by: eva-md/<version>}`, status: stable, then locked, remote, writer.
- **Body:** `## Threads` has one line per thread, newest activity first: `* [<branch>](threads/<file>.md) — <first sentence of its summary> · last active <date>`. Every thread is listed, so okf never finds one orphaned. Folding idle threads and marking merged or gone ones are not in v1. Once the folder has a rules page, a `## Rules` section before `## Threads` links it (`* [Standing rules](rules.md)`), so okf finds no orphan there either.

### Rules page

Standing rules are what the user asked to hold from now on, in the user's own words. `projects/<slug>/rules.md` holds a project's, and `general/rules.md` the rules for all projects. The background update writes them (see Validation 12), and a person may edit them.
- **Frontmatter:** type `rules`, title `<slug> · rules` or `General · rules`.
- **Body:** `# <title>`, then `## Rules`: one rule a line in the thread item format, `- <text> [stated] <refs> <!-- i:r1 t:<time> -->`, at most 40, newest last. Ids are `r<k>` on a project's page and `g<k>` on the general page, so one pass can name rules on both. An edit replaces a rule and moves it to the end. A dropped rule moves, struck through, to `## Retired rules` as `- ~~<text>~~ (dropped <date>: <reason>) [stated] <refs> <!-- i:r1 ... -->`; that section isn't shown in briefs and has no size limit, and new ids count past it, so an id is never reused.
- **Hand edits:** any non-blank line under `## Rules` is a rule, and one without a usable id gets one at the next write; other sections are kept byte for byte. A heading or fence among the rules, text before the first section other than the title, a second `## Rules` or another type marks the page damaged, and it is never rewritten. Locked and verified pages are left alone too.
- **In the brief:** startup, clear and compact briefs start with the rules under "Standing rules, the user's own words, newest first:", those for all projects first, each with its tag and refs, at most 600 characters, reserved like the footer. Trimmed from the end, the block closes with `N more: <bin> show general/rules`, or the project's page, or both. The briefed marker's hash becomes `<Now hash>+<rules hash>` when rules exist (older markers read as before), so a changed rule triggers a refresh, headed "Standing rules (a rule changed since this chat last saw them), newest first:", which spends one of the chat's 2 refreshes; a resumed or forked chat gets changed rules too.

### System page

One page per system (a service, a repo, a platform or a major documentation area), `systems/<slug>.md`, shared by every project in the wiki. An agent writes it, usually through the digest skill (see Packaging), with `eva-md system write <slug> [--replace] < page.md`. The slug is 2 to 64 characters of `[a-z0-9-]` starting with a letter; `map`, `index`, `log` and `agents` are reserved. The write checks the page line by line and saves it through the adapter with the map, the folder index and the names index, under one hold of the wiki lock (`internal/systempage`).

- **Frontmatter:** `type: system`, `title` (at most 80 characters), `description` (at most 160), `generated: { by: <host>/<model> }`, `status: stable`, `sources` (at least one), `as_of` (YYYY-MM-DD, no later than tomorrow, which leaves room for time zones), `state: active`, and optionally `repos` (git remotes or project keys, stored as project keys), `aliases` (at most 10, of 3 to 60 characters) and `stale_after` (YYYY-MM-DD, the day to review a page built only from documentation, which eva-md can't check; it may be in the future, and okf saves it before the sources). Unknown or repeated keys are refused, and quoted values lose their outer spaces. The write saves `repos` and `aliases` as flow lists (`aliases: ["order service"]`), also with `--replace` over a key a person rewrote as a block list or a mapping. A title, description or alias may not hold ` · `, `](` or a speaker label.
- **Sources:** an `https://` URL without credentials, optionally with its version (a query parameter, or ` v<n>` after the URL), or `<repo>@<commit>[:<path>]` with a commit of 7 to 40 hex digits and a relative path to a file or a folder, a folder's trailing `/` dropped and a path refused when it has an empty, `.` or `..` part (`./x`, `x/./y`, `x/.` and `.` included) or holds `](`, a space, a control or format character, or bytes that are not UTF-8. System status and `eva-md show` print such a character in a path, a clone or a branch, as a status file an older eva-md wrote may hold, as `?`; a plain space prints as it is. A page cites at most 16 code sources, refused with "cite the folders a page describes rather than every file", and any number of documents; it may cite several commits of one repo. A URL is refused when a query parameter's name, or a part of it split on `-`, `_` or `.`, is sig, signature, token, key, apikey, secret, password, pwd or auth.
- **Body:** `## Summary` (required, at most 800 characters), then optionally, in this order, `## Responsibilities`, `## Interfaces`, `## Depends on`, `## Used by`, `## Decisions and constraints`, `## Open questions` and `## Unverified`, for what no source shows. `###` headings may sit inside them; no other heading. A page is at most 24 KB as written; the code pass, system status, maintain and `eva-md show` read a saved page up to 25 KB (`systempage.MaxSaved`), for the lines the save adds, so a page written at its cap is measured. Links go to `https://` pages or to system pages that exist. Code ends the page with `Part of the [system map](map.md).`
- **Map:** `systems/map.md`, built by code, links every page, one line each, sorted by slug: `- [<title>](<slug>.md): <description> · repos: <key>, <key> · code: <key>@<commit>[:<path>], <key>@<commit>[:<path>] · as of <YYYY-MM-DD> · review by <YYYY-MM-DD>`, each field after the description only when it has a value. `code:` lists the page's code sources, each once, in its order, with the repo as its project key; `review by` is its `stale_after`. Its repos are the page's own and then the repos of its code sources, so a page that cites a project's code is that project's in the brief, and `code:` never directly follows the description: an eva-md from before code refs, which takes the description to end at the first field it knows, reads the line and skips the fields it doesn't. A ` · ` in a hand-edited value is shown as ` - `. The folder index, `systems/index.md`, lists the pages and the map, one line each by file name, `* [<title>](<slug>.md) - <description>`; the brief, the prompt's reminder, `eva_status`, `eva_show`'s description and the recall skill point readers at it, since its line says what a system is without the repos and every code source a map line adds (30 systems citing 6 sources each take about 4.7 KB there and 23 KB on the map), while the brief, the prompt hook, the code status and the digest skill read the map for its repos and code. A write saves the folder index before the map, which decides whether anything points at it, so a write stopped between the two never leaves a map without its index. The root index gains `* [Systems](systems/index.md) - The systems the projects share` at the first write.
- **No repos.** A page that lists no repo and cites no code has a map line that names no project, so no brief and no `eva_status` points at it; search still finds it, and a prompt that names it still gets a pointer. The write saves it, and prints after the `wrote` line, for information only: `no repos: no project's brief or eva_status points at this page, which lists no repo and cites no code; to have them point at it, list the repos that build or use the system under repos and write it again with --replace`.
- **Never written.** The page is refused, with each problem named by its line and never its text (at most 20), when it exists and `--replace` is not given, changed since it was read, is human-verified, is locked (`locked: true`, `yes` or `on`, in any case) or can't be read, or the map can't be read. Its content is refused for a carriage return or another control or format character, a comment or fence, a `[REDACTED:` marker, a heading out of the list or order, a link to a system with no page yet, a bad key or source, or a line the daily commit's secret scan flags, checked in the page, in the frontmatter as it will be saved (named by the key's line) and in its map and index lines, or a code source whose path the cited commit lacks in a local clone (see "Code a system page cites"). The exists, verified or locked reason comes in the same refusal as the content problems.
- **Names index.** Every write rebuilds `.eva-md/names.tsv` under the same lock, writing it only when it changes: `<name>`, the page id and the title, tab-separated, one name a line, sorted by name. The names are each page's title, slug and aliases, with control characters as spaces, runs of spaces collapsed, punctuation trimmed from the ends and the case kept. At most 2,000 names and 128 KB: past them the aliases of the pages with the oldest `as_of` go first (a page without one counts as oldest), and only then those pages' other names. The doctor writes it again when it is missing or out of date.
- **Repos index.** Every write also rebuilds `.eva-md/repos.tsv` under the same lock, writing it only when it changes: the header `# eva-md repos index 1`, then one line for each page that lists two or more repos, its own and those of its code sources as on the map, each once: the page id and the project keys, tab-separated, sorted by id. A page with one repo makes no project another's sibling and has no line, and with no such page there is no file: the write that leaves none removes it, and the doctor removes an empty one. At most 64 KB: past it the pages with the oldest `as_of` are left out (a page without one counts as oldest). Prompt recall reads it to learn which projects share a system page with the chat's (see "Per prompt"). The doctor writes it again, with the names index, when it is missing or out of date, and its system names row covers both, naming the index at fault.

### Code a system page cites

A system page's code sources are its tie to the code: no other key holds it, and staleness is measured, never written into a page or the map. `internal/coderef` does the measuring, with git calls hardened as identity's are and pathspecs read literally; it never fetches and never writes into a clone. Lazy fetches are off: every git call runs with `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, and in a clone that may be partial it also passes git's `--no-lazy-fetch`. A clone may be partial when its config or `config.worktree` has a `promisor` or `partialclonefilter` key, `extensions.partialclone`, or an `include` or `includeIf` section, read by their names, never by a word in a value such as a remote's URL, or a line continued with a backslash or a section header it can't read; or when its git folder is not in the form git writes; eva-md reads those files without starting git. A git older than 2.44 ignores the variable and refuses the option, so in such a clone the call fails and reads as git failing; it never fetches. No signature is checked (`log.showSignature=false`). A call that runs out of time is killed together with every process it started.

- **Clones.** eva-md finds a repo's clones through the identity cache, which holds an entry for every worktree a chat ran in: newest entry first, the working folder's own project first of all, at most 8 a repo listed (of at most 4,096 entries of at most 16 KB each) and 3 tried for each commit. Each is resolved again, through the cache, before use, so a folder that is gone or now holds another repo is never measured. The cache holds an entry for a worktree under each git build and environment that resolved it; the finder lists each worktree once, by its newest entry, so one clone never takes more than one of the 3 tries. A repo never chatted in on this Mac has no clone. Hooks never look for clones.
- **At write.** `eva-md system write` checks, within 3 s and 1 s a git call, each repo and commit the page cites in a local clone, the folder it runs in first: `cat-file -e` for the commit, then one `ls-tree <commit> -- <paths>`. A path the commit lacks refuses the page, naming the source's line, as a typo or an invented file would. A commit the clones lack (not fetched, rebased away or cut off by a shallow clone), a repo with no clone, git failing and the time running out are notes, printed after the `wrote` line with the clone checked in, so a write never waits on a clone. Nothing about movement is measured then.
- **Measured.** For a repo and commit a page cites, eva-md tries its clones in order and moves to the next one only when a clone lacks the commit. Git failing or running out of time in a clone ends the search there, recorded as `git` or `timeout`. In the clone it settles on: `rev-list --count --max-count=1001 <commit>..HEAD -- <paths>` (the whole repo when the page cites it so), which counts the commits that touch those paths up to 1,000 ("1000+"); `merge-base --is-ancestor`, since a commit off HEAD's history is counted from the commit the two have in common; `ls-tree HEAD -- <paths>` for the paths gone at HEAD, as after a rename, which also stops the count growing; and, for printing only, the newest change's day and subject from `log -1`, redacted, on one line and at most 80 characters.
- **The status.** `eva-md system status [<slug>...] [--json]` and the daily job measure every page, 10 s a call, 120 s a pass and at most 64 repo and commit pairs (past them, a pair is recorded as not run), and save `<state>/code-status.json` (0600, derived, local to this Mac and never committed): for each page its as_of and review day, and for each repo and commit its paths, its state (`unchanged`, `moved`, `not_on_branch`, `unknown_commit`, `no_clone`, `timeout`, `git` or `not_run`), the count, the paths gone at HEAD, and the clone, branch and HEAD it was measured at; the pages that couldn't be read; and how many pages cite code their map line lacks, as one written before the map carried code does until it is written again. Never a commit subject. With slugs, only those pages' entries change: a named page that can no longer be read loses its old entry and is listed as unreadable, and a later pass that reads it again takes it off that list. System status prints a line for each repo and commit, most moved first, such as `orders: code moved 14 commits since 2026-10-30 (github.com/acme/orders at 4e2bf4c: internal/orders, api/orders.proto; measured in <clone> on main; gone at HEAD: api/orders.proto; last change 2026-10-29: <subject>)` or `billing: not checked: no clone of github.com/acme/billing is known on this Mac; eva-md learns clones from chats in them`, a line for each page past its review day, `manual: review due since 2026-09-30`, then the counts. An entry that is unchanged while HEAD's tree lacks some of its paths means the cited commit lacks them too, since a removal or rename since would have been counted; a path cited with a typo is the usual case. System status and show lead its line with `not counted` and word it `cites a path its commit lacks: <paths>` rather than `gone at HEAD`, and no prompt pointer, search hit or `eva_status` says such code is unchanged, nor the brief's pointer once the code status has measured the page (see "Code in the pointers"). Likewise, a commit off HEAD's history with nothing counted since the two forked gives no movement, and its line leads with `not counted`: the commit is not on this branch, with nothing counted since the commit the two have in common. System status sorts both kinds with the pages that couldn't be checked, and its counts line adds `; N cite a path its commit lacks` and `; N cite a commit off its clone's branch`. It exits 0 whatever the code did.
- **eva-md show.** After a system page or a section of it, `show` prints the same lines on stderr, each starting `eva.md: systems/<slug>: `, measured live within 3 s, 1 s a git call. Then one line names the system pages that link to the page, sorted, at most 12 and how many more: `eva.md: systems/<slug>: linked from systems/billing, systems/payments`. It reads the other system pages, never the map, up to 2,000 and each up to 25 KB, and counts their links as markdown reads them and system write lets a page link: a relative `<slug>.md`, perhaps with a `#section`, outside code spans. Stdout stays the page alone, so a page shown is written back byte for byte. The map, other refs, and a page that cites no code, has no review due and no page links to get no line.
- **Where it shows.** The brief's pointers count the project's own code live (see "System pointers"); a prompt's pointers read the status (see "Per prompt"); the doctor's `system code` row reads it (see "Health check"). A system page's search hits and `eva_status`'s systems read it too, with the system map, never git, and say it only where a prompt's pointer would: a hit after its ref, without the day it already shows, as in `[system] 2026-10-28 · systems/orders#Interfaces · code moved 14 commits · <snippet>`, and `eva_status` after the slug, as in `orders (code moved 14 commits since 2026-10-30)`. A search reads them once, and only when a system page's line is among the hits it prints. A review day is shown by show, system status and the doctor, never in a hook's pointer.

### Knowledge page

One page per lasting fact, decision, procedure or constraint, `knowledge/k<N>.md`, shared by every project in the wiki. Only the background update writes one (see Validation 13); `eva-md doctor` and the daily maintain build again the files that list them (see "Health check" and "Daily commit and push"). `internal/knowledgepage` reads, renders and saves the pages, and every write goes through the adapter.

- **Ids.** N is a decimal number from 1, without leading zeros, of at most 9 digits. A new page takes one more than the largest N of any `k<N>.md` entry in the folder, retired pages and entries that are not regular files included, so an id is never used again. The id is `k<N>` and the ref `knowledge/k<N>`.
- **Kinds:** `fact`, `decision`, `procedure` and `constraint`, a standing limit on the work or the system: what must or must not be done, or must hold. The brief lists constraints.
- **Scope:** `project`, for the project it was learned in, or `all`, for every project. Every page records the project key it was learned in, `general` in the general area.
- **State:** `active` or `retired`. A retired page stays, listed on the map.

```
---
type: knowledge
title: Inbox format
description: The inbox stores entries as JSONL, one file per session
generated: { by: claude-code/claude-opus-5-5, at: "2026-11-02T09:30:00Z" }
status: stable
sources:
  - resource: "s:3f2a91c0#14"
  - resource: "github.com__acme__eva@4e2bf4c:internal/inbox"
as_of: "2026-10-28"
kind: "decision"
project: "github.com__acme__eva"
scope: "project"
state: "active"
---

## Statement
The inbox stores entries as JSONL, one file per session [stated] s:3f2a91c0#14 <!-- t:2026-10-28T10:00:00+01:00 -->

## History

Part of the [knowledge map](map.md).
```

- **Frontmatter.** okf writes its keys in its fixed order, then eva-md's flat extra keys sorted: `as_of`, `kind`, `project`, `scope` and `state`. The title is at most 80 characters, and the description holds the statement, at most 300, on one line; the body holds the same text, so an index line and okf's drift check see it. `generated.by` is the update's `<host>/<model>`. `as_of` is the local date, in the entry's own offset, of the newest entry the statement cites.
- **Sources.** The inbox refs of every statement the page has held, at most the last 20, then its code sources, `<project key>@<commit>:<path>`, at most 3, at the commit the batch's HEAD names, each one that `coderef.Parse` reads back. A page of the general area, or one written from a batch with no commit, cites no code, and its paths are counted as dropped.
- **Body.** `## Statement` holds one line: the statement, its tag, its refs and a hidden comment with the time of the newest entry it cites, against which a later supersede or drop is checked. `## History` follows once it has lines, oldest first: `- ~~<old>~~ → <new> (superseded YYYY-MM-DD) [<tag>] <refs>` for a supersede and `- ~~<statement>~~ (retired YYYY-MM-DD: <reason>) [<tag>] <refs>` for a drop. Code ends the page with `Part of the [knowledge map](map.md).` No section is named Timeline or Now, which the MCP server's cut hint reads as a thread page's.
- **Escaping.** The title, statement, reason and history go through the thread page escaper (`threadpage.CleanLine`), so no text can forge a heading, a fence, a comment, a link, a tag or a frontmatter key, and a `---` in a title or statement becomes `--`, since okf refuses it there. A page whose title or Statement line a person edited to hold `---` or a line break, a JSON-quoted title that unquotes to one included, reads as damaged: it is never written, an operation on it is skipped with reason `damaged` and the rest of the save goes on, and it stays listed read-only in the recall index.
- **Never written.** A page that is locked (`locked: true`, `yes` or `on`, in any case), verified, of another type or can't be read back safely is never written: the operation on it is skipped with that reason. A page is verified when okf reads a `verified` entry from it, or its frontmatter has a top-level `verified` key with any value but an empty one, inline or in the lines under it, so `verified: true`, a plain value and a block mapping count too, though okf reads no entry from them and drops the key when it writes (`okfadapter.IsVerified`, which thread, rules and system pages use too). Text eva-md doesn't know in the Statement or History section, or before the first section, makes a page damaged, and so does a body okf's save would refuse: a line that trims to `---` opens or closes a block, and a line in such a block that starts with `type`, `status`, `generated`, `verified`, `governance`, `code_refs` or `stale_after` and a colon, in any case, code fence or not (`okfadapter.SmugglesFrontmatter`), so the operation is skipped rather than failing the pass. Other `##` sections a person adds, extra frontmatter keys and extra sources are kept through a write.
- **A damaged page is still listed.** A page okf reads as `type: knowledge` whose frontmatter values eva-md reads, but whose body it can't read back safely, is read leniently, as search reads a page: text eva-md doesn't know is passed over, and the statement is the first statement line with its tag, or else the first one without, or else the description. It is listed on the map and in the folder index by its title, counted by its kind, held against a repeated add, and listed in the recall index as `read-only`, with no tag when its statement has none, so it is still recalled and, as a constraint, listed by the brief; it is never written.
- **Saving.** `knowledgepage.Save` holds the wiki lock once: it reads every page, applies the pass's operations in order, then writes the map, `knowledge/index.md` and the recall index, and adds the root index's line once, each only when it changes. Running the same operations again writes nothing: an add whose statement, as lowercased words of letters and digits, an active page that applies to the save's project (one for all projects, or the project's own) already holds is a duplicate, a locked, verified or damaged one included, a supersede the page already holds is the same, and a drop of a retired page is passed over. Another project's own page with the same statement is no duplicate: the statement may hold here too. A supersede may change the kind, the title and the scope, and its paths replace the code sources when they give at least one; when the update narrows a page for all projects to the chat's project, the operation names that project and the page moves to it. A compare-and-swap conflict is tried again, up to 3 tries in all. When an operation fails after the save wrote a page, the map, the folder index and the recall index are still built for the pages written, and then the error is returned. A save that writes no page creates no folder. Each page saved adds its line to log.md, as any page does.
- **Map.** `knowledge/map.md`, of `type: map`, titled `Knowledge map` with the description `Every knowledge page, one line each`, built by code with actor `eva-md/<version>`, lists every page in id order, retired ones too, so none is orphaned: `- [<title>](k<N>.md): <kind> · <this project | all projects> · <project key> · as of <YYYY-MM-DD>`, with ` · retired` after a retired page's line. A page okf can't read, or one of another type, or a damaged page whose frontmatter eva-md can't read either, is listed as `- [k<N> (unreadable)](k<N>.md)`. A map a person locked or verified, or one okf can't read as a map, is left as it is: the pages are still written, and the doctor says why the map is out of date. The map is read without a page's 256 KiB bound, since it grows with the pages: a large map is out of date at worst, never damaged.
- **Folder and root index.** `knowledge/index.md` lists the map, every page and every other regular `.md` page a person put in the folder under `# Knowledge`, in okf's line format, a person's page by its title and description as okf reads them, or by its file name, so a save never drops its line. The root index gains `* [Knowledge](knowledge/index.md) - Lasting facts, decisions, procedures and constraints from earlier chats` at the first knowledge page, and loses it once the folder is gone.
- **The recall index.** `.eva-md/knowledge.tsv` (0600) is built from the pages by every save, by the doctor's repair and by maintain, and written only when it is out of date: when its bytes differ, or when the strict read below refuses it, as for a file with a second hard link, which the write replaces. It is a cache of the pages: deleting it loses nothing that a rebuild doesn't restore. When a person removes `knowledge/`, a recall index or root index line left from it is out of date: the repair leaves the index its header alone and removes the line, without making the folder again. Its first line is `# eva-md knowledge index 1`, then one line per active page, newest `as_of` first, then the newest cited entry, then the highest id, with 11 tab-separated fields: `k<N>`, the kind, the tag (empty only on a `read-only` row whose page lost its tag to a person's edit), the scope, the project key, the as_of, the time of the newest cited entry, `read-only` for a page a person locked or verified, or a damaged page read leniently (or nothing), the title, the statement, and the code paths separated by commas. Tabs, line breaks and control characters in a field become spaces, and runs of spaces one. It holds at most 512 KiB: past it, the pages with the oldest `as_of` are left out, still pages that search finds, but neither recalled nor listed. Retired pages are never in it. A reader skips a line that is not such a row, and reads nothing from a file without that header.
- **The strict read.** The prompt hook and the brief read the recall index by its path alone, through the wiki's pinned root: a regular file with no link anywhere on its path and one hard link, at most 512 KiB, read up to 512 KiB and one byte. They never list `knowledge/` or read a page. The update's plan and `eva_status` read it the same way.

### Branches and threads

- The thread key is the branch read live from the payload's `cwd`.
- A detached HEAD keeps the session's last thread. A repo with no commits and the general area use `default`.
- If a session switches branch midway, its entries are grouped by the branch recorded on each entry.
- A new branch with no thread uses the lineage fallback (see Resume).

### Schema versioning

The binary declares the highest schema it supports.
- If the wiki is newer, eva-md goes read-only: capture and briefs continue, writes and commits stop (the daily commit is refused with reason `schema`), and a notice appears.
- If the wiki is older, `eva-md migrate` (later; not built) will preview and then apply in one commit.
- Unknown frontmatter keys and sections are kept.

---

## The inbox

**Location.** `~/.local/state/eva-md/inbox/<project-key>/<host>-<session-id>.jsonl` on each machine.
- The project key is the normalized remote (`github.com__you__eva-md`), `local-<folder>-<hash6>`, or `general`. When a name would read as a provider token, the key is `repo-<hash12>` or `local-<hash12>` instead, so the redactor never rewrites it. Which names read so depends on the current rules, so a rule change can move a project to the other form; its later chats then go to the new key's folder.
- Watermarks live in `inbox/<key>/.state/`. Session markers live in `~/.local/state/eva-md/sessions/`, heartbeats in `heartbeats/` (each event's last 20 runs, and each host's `uncaptured.json`, the time a hook last ran in a folder eva-md doesn't capture) and transcript cursors and drift records in `catchup/` under the same folder; so do the background update's run log and the day it last took the day's sweep (`sweep`), in `updates/`, its detached jobs' logs, in `logs/`, the daily commit's records, in `commits/`, and its trigger markers, in `commit/`, the lint's `lint.json` and install's `cli-paths.json`. The identity cache lives in `~/.local/state/eva-md/cache/` and can be deleted at any time.
- The search index lives in `~/.local/state/eva-md/cache/search/`, a folder per inbox project folder. It is built from the inbox, never committed, and can be deleted at any time (see "Search index").
- Folders are 0700 and files 0600 (umask 077). The inbox is kept outside the wiki, every repo, and Documents, Desktop and iCloud. Capture works before the wiki exists.

**Format.** JSONL, one object per line, never rewritten. Fields:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `v` | format version, 1 |
| `ts` | RFC 3339 with local offset |
| `host` | `claude-code` or `codex` |
| `session` | host session id |
| `turn` | Claude `prompt_id` or Codex `turn_id`. Absent on Codex SessionStart and SessionEnd, and before the first prompt on Claude |
| `role` | `user`, `assistant`, `tool`, `plan`, `host` or `event` |
| `kind` | see below |
| `agent` | subagent id when the payload carries `agent_id` |
| `text` | redacted text |
| `project`, `branch`, `cwd` | identity. `cwd` is relative to the home folder |
| `model` | when the payload has it (Codex: every event except SessionEnd) |
| `origin` | `hook` or `catchup` |
| `red` | redaction count |
| `trunc` | bytes cut from the middle of anything over 200 KB |

Kinds:
- **prompt:** your words, verbatim.
- **reply:** the final assistant message, verbatim. On Claude this is `last_assistant_message`, which holds only the final reply text. Codex's value may be null, which counts as a missing reply.
- **tool:** tool name, plus what it acted on, plus the exit code, never the output: the file path for Read, Edit, Write and NotebookEdit, the redacted head of a command, the quoted pattern and the path for Grep and Glob, the URL for WebFetch without its user part, its fragment or any query parameter whose name says it carries a credential (such as `sig`, `token`, `key` or `code`), the file names of an `apply_patch`, at most 64, and for an `mcp__*` tool its string and number arguments as `key=value`, shortest first, at most 16, leaving out a value over 1 KiB and any argument whose name says it carries a credential (such as `auth`, `cookie`, `pass`, `password`, `session` or `apiKey`), and cleaning an argument that is an absolute URL as WebFetch's URL is, when the argument is named as an address (url, uri, href, link or address, even with a space in the path) or the URL is the whole argument (text that only starts with an address is kept as it is). A WebSearch is kept by its name alone, never its query or results, so the update can tell a turn that read the web. Each part is redacted; a command head, a pattern and the MCP arguments are cut to 200 characters, a path or URL to 1024. The Claude Code plugin's tool matcher includes Read, Grep, Glob, WebFetch, WebSearch and `mcp__` tools, and the Codex plugin's includes `mcp__` tools, so these lines come from the hooks themselves.
  - Claude: success (PostToolUse) means exit 0. Failures come from PostToolUseFailure, whose error starts with "Exit code N".
  - Codex: parse `Exit code: N` when present and discard the response text. On Codex 0.164.0, a unified-shell command with no output, such as `exit 7`, returns an empty response and no other exit-status field, so record `exit unknown` when it is missing. Never infer success from turn completion or the requested command. File paths are parsed from apply_patch's `*** Add/Update/Delete File:` lines.
  - Subagent tool lines carry `agent` and are labelled "(subagent)".
- **plan:** a checklist from Claude's TaskCreate, TaskUpdate or TodoWrite tool calls when present, or from Codex `update_plan`. On current Claude default models these tools are off unless you opt in, so Claude plan entries are optional.
