[releasing.md](releasing.md) is the maintainer's guide; this is the contract.

- **Tag.** A release's tag is its version: the UTC day of the released commit's commit date as `YYYY.M.D` without zero padding, then `.2`, `.3` and so on for later releases the same day, with no `v` prefix: `^[0-9]{4}\.[1-9][0-9]?\.[1-9][0-9]?(\.[2-9]|\.[1-9][0-9]+)?$`. A tag is annotated, its message is the release notes (the subjects of the commits since the previous release tag), and it is never moved, deleted or reused. A tag cut from a Mac is signed; one cut from GitHub is unsigned, and the approval GitHub records for it stands in for the signature.
- **Cutting one.** There are two ways, the Cut a release workflow on GitHub and `scripts/release.sh` from a Mac, and both make the same checks, with the script. It checks, in this order, that it runs from the checkout's root on main, with a clean tree and no untracked files, that HEAD carries the CI and release workflows, both install scripts and the licenses, that tag signing is set up, that origin and any push URL it has are `github.com/heyNag/eva-md`, that HEAD is origin/main after a fetch, that gh is signed in to github.com, that CI passed for HEAD and that HEAD isn't released yet (a release tag on HEAD that only this clone has stops with the command that pushes it); it stops at the first failure with its fix. It names the tag and shows the tag, commit and notes. Its git calls that format commits run with `log.showSignature=false`, so a signature check can't reach the tag's name or the notes. Run as `--plan`, the script is the one place the tag and notes are worked out for a release from GitHub: it makes every check but the signing check, checks that gh can read the repository (`gh api repos/heyNag/eva-md`) instead of that it is signed in, since a workflow's gh signs in with a token `gh auth status` may not recognise, asks nothing, and when `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` is set writes the tag, the commit, the previous release and the notes there, the notes between lines of a random delimiter. `--plan` and `--dry-run` can't be combined. In a workflow (`GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`), each failure is also printed to stdout as an error command, `::error::<problem>. Fix: <fix>`, with `%`, CR and LF encoded, so a failed plan shows as an annotation on the run.
- **From GitHub.** `.github/workflows/cut-release.yml`, Cut a release, is run by hand (`workflow_dispatch`, with a boolean `dry_run`). Its jobs run in a line, each needing the one before: **plan** fails off main, when main moved since the run started, and unless the repository's `release` environment exists with at least one required reviewer (it warns when the environment allows any branch), then runs `scripts/release.sh --plan` and writes the tag, a link to the commit and the notes to the run's summary; a dry run stops there. **approve** runs in the `release` environment, so GitHub holds it until a required reviewer approves and records who did; that approval is in place of a signature. **tag** checks the plan's tag and commit, makes an unsigned annotated tag on the planned commit with the notes, its tagger who started the run, pushes only that tag, never with force, and checks origin has that tag. When origin has the tag already, the job makes and pushes none and goes on only when origin's tag is annotated, on the planned commit and has the planned notes, as when its run's failed jobs are re-run after a failure that followed the push; otherwise it fails, since a tag is never moved. **publish** calls the release workflow with the tag. **check**, on macOS, checks the published release as the script does, and also that each asset's provenance names the planned commit (`--source-digest`); its source ref is `refs/heads/main`, since a called workflow's source ref is its caller's. Its provenance failure says to re-run the run's failed jobs first, and to make the release a draft and release a fix if it fails again. The workflow may do nothing by default; plan may read Actions and contents, approve nothing, tag write contents, publish grant exactly what the release workflow's jobs ask for (`contents: write`, `id-token: write`, `attestations: write`), and check read contents and attestations. Every job but publish has a time limit, only the tag job pushes and only its checkout keeps credentials, one run goes at a time, and every action is pinned to a full commit SHA. A dry run's run is named "Cut a release (dry run)" and a release's "Cut a release" (`run-name`), so the runs list tells them apart. No job's name holds an expression, since GitHub doesn't evaluate the name of a job it skips: the approve and tag jobs, which a dry run skips, are "Approve the release" and "Tag the release", and the plan's summary names the tag.
- **From a Mac.** `scripts/release.sh`, the fallback, asks `Release <tag> from <short commit>? [y/N]` on the terminal, reading the answer from the terminal, never stdin; `--dry-run` stops before the question. After a yes it makes the signed tag, pushes only that tag, watches the release workflow's run with `gh run watch --exit-status`, then downloads the release and checks every asset, `SHA256SUMS`, each asset's build provenance, with the tag as its source ref (`--source-ref refs/tags/<tag>`), and then the version this Mac's binary prints, so nothing downloaded runs before its provenance verifies, and prints the release's URL and the install command.
- **Workflow.** `.github/workflows/release.yml` runs on a pushed tag, and when Cut a release calls it with the tag (`workflow_call`), since a tag pushed with a workflow's token starts no workflow; called, it reads the tag from its input, and either way one run per tag goes at a time. It refuses a tag not in the format, not annotated, whose commit isn't on main, or whose release exists; builds both darwin binaries with `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `-trimpath`, `-mod=readonly` and the tag as `main.version`; copies the install scripts and the licenses from the tagged tree; writes `SHA256SUMS`; checks the sums and the arm64 binary's version on a macOS runner; attests the five files' build provenance, whose signer is `release.yml` whichever way the release was cut; and creates the release with the tag's annotation as its notes, followed, when Cut a release called it, by a line linking the run, the caller's, which records the approval; the tag doesn't name the run. gh matches `--signer-workflow` as a prefix, so every provenance check also names the source ref. The provenance proves the files were built by `release.yml` from the commit, not who released them: a Mac tag's signature or a GitHub run's approval shows that. Each job has only the permissions it needs and a time limit, checkout keeps no credentials, and every action is pinned to a full commit SHA.
- **Assets.** `eva-md-darwin-arm64` and `eva-md-darwin-amd64` (plain executables), `install.sh`, `uninstall.sh`, `LICENSES.txt` and `SHA256SUMS`, one `shasum -a 256` line (`<64 hex>  <name>`) for each of the other five. `LICENSES.txt` is `scripts/LICENSES.txt`: eva-md's license, then the license file of each module the binaries are built with, the license of Go, whose runtime and standard library they contain, and the notices in Go's own code that ask to go with a copy of the program (fdlibm's in package math, and Inferno's in the Intel binary's runtime), since each of those licenses and notices asks to go with every copy. They are at `https://github.com/heyNag/eva-md/releases/download/<tag>/<name>`, and the newest release's at `https://github.com/heyNag/eva-md/releases/latest/download/<name>`; `https://github.com/heyNag/eva-md/releases/latest` redirects to the newest release's tag page.

---

## Command-line surface

| Command | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `eva-md setup [--dir <folder>] [--remote <url>] [--git-host <url>] [--host claude\|codex\|all] [--no-push] [--yes]` | First-run setup in a terminal: checks this Mac, stopping with the fix at the first thing missing, then asks for the wiki folder and remote, unless `~/.config/eva-md/config.env` gives them, then init, install and the health check; `--git-host` names, by any web address on it, the host where it may create the repo, with gh or glab as the host is GitHub or GitLab |
| `eva-md init --remote <url> --dir <folder> [--no-push]` | Clone or adopt the empty wiki repo, scaffold it through okf, validate, make the first signed commit, push, and write the per-machine config |
| `eva-md validate [--dir <folder>]` | Run okf Validate on the wiki (the configured one by default) and print counts only. Exits 1 on errors, orphans, broken links, or actor and parent-index warnings |
| `eva-md install [--host claude\|codex\|all] [--dry-run]` | Place the binary, generate both marketplaces, install and verify through the host CLIs, roll back only this run's steps on failure; then link `~/.local/bin/eva-md` when that folder is on PATH, and add to the install record |
| `eva-md uninstall [--apply] [--all] [--yes]` | Preview by default. `--apply` removes plugins, marketplace entries, marketplace folders, the binary and the link, in that order, keeping the inbox, config and wiki; `--all` also removes the config and the local data after `delete` is typed on a terminal, `--yes` or not, or with `--yes` without one. Never the wiki |
| `eva-md upgrade [--check] [--force] [--version TAG]` | Install the latest release, or `--version`, when it is newer than this binary, by downloading and checking it and running its setup; `--check` only says whether one is newer, `--force` installs one that isn't (see "The install scripts and upgrade") |
| `eva-md doctor [--json] [--live]` | Health report: PASS, FAIL or UNKNOWN on each line, with a one-line fix (see "Health check"). It has two repairs: it writes the names and repos indexes again when either is missing or out of date, and builds the knowledge map, folder index, recall index and root index line again from the pages when any is. `--live` runs the exact headless update command for each installed host on a fixture |
| `eva-md status [--json]` | Local health without querying the hosts, plus the wiki's contents (projects, threads and knowledge pages) and the update backlog. It never writes: a missing or stale names or repos index, or knowledge files out of date, are a FAIL |
| `eva-md brief [--cwd DIR] [--as startup\|clear\|compact\|resume\|fork] [--session ID] [--host claude-code\|codex]` | Print exactly what a new chat would receive |
| `eva-md show <s:id#line \| page[#section]> [--last N] [--since YYYY-MM-DD]` | Resolve a source ref to inbox entries, or print a page or one section of it. A section is streamed with bounded memory and ends where `threadpage.Parse` ends it, so a heading in a fenced block ends nothing. `#Now` and other named sections stop reading at the section's end. `#Timeline` shows the newest 10 date groups, or those `--last N` (1 or more) or `--since` choose, and `#<YYYY-MM-DD>` that day's group; both read the page once and stop at about 16 KB with a count of the lines left. A section is found by its heading or its anchor, in any case. A whole page over 2 MiB is refused. A wiki page is cleaned as the brief cleans text, but a line keeps its identifiers when the daily commit's scan passes it and it cleans the same alone as within the whole page, so a value made suspect by the line before stays redacted; a line holding an escape sequence is shown as plain cleaning leaves it. After a system page or a section of it, a line on stderr for each repo and commit the page cites, saying how far its code moved since the page's as_of and where that was measured, or why it couldn't be, and one once its review day has come, measured within 3 s (see "Code a system page cites"), then one naming the system pages that link to it; stdout stays the page. A knowledge page (`knowledge/k<N>`, its `#Statement` and `#History`) and `knowledge/map` show as any page, with nothing on stderr |
| `eva-md search <terms...> [--all] [--days N] [--limit N] [--cwd DIR] [--pages \| --inbox]` | Find earlier chat text in the inbox and lines of the thread, system and knowledge pages (`--pages` or `--inbox` for one of them): this project and the general area, or every project with `--all`; the last 90 days unless `--days` says otherwise (0 for no limit), though system and knowledge pages are always searched; the 10 best hits, or up to 50 with `--limit`, each with a ref for `eva-md show`. Run by the agent, never by a hook |
| `eva-md update [--session host:id ... \| --pending] [--sweep] [--dry-run] [--foreground]` | Run the background update for the sessions named (`--session` once or more), or for every session with a backlog, under one global lock and the daily cap. `--sweep`, alone or with `--session` but not with `--pending` or `--dry-run`, also takes the day's sweep, at most once a day: up to 5 sessions of the whole inbox idle an hour or more with a backlog, newest first (see "Triggers"); a person's chat start adds it to its catch-up. `--foreground` runs attached with progress on stderr, as a person's manual run; without it, the lines go to the day's log. `--dry-run` takes one session, prints the next pass's exact command, environment names, rules, input and schema, and makes no call. The triggers spawn it detached |
| `eva-md maintain [--push-only]` | The daily job the hooks spawn: the knowledge files built again when they are out of date, the day's commit and push, unless `commits = false`, then the lint, the code status of the system pages, the pruning of old attempt markers and of the transcript-recovery records not saved for 30 days or past a host's newest 2,048, then the search index step, under its own lock (a busy run exits 0); without a wiki, only the search index step and the pruning. `--push-only` only pushes, while the newest commit record's push is pending. At most one line a step (the knowledge step only when it rebuilt something, or found the wiki busy and left it for the next run, and the records' pruning only when it removed some); exits 1 when a step was refused or failed |
| `eva-md index [--rebuild] [--json]` | Bring the search index up to date with the inbox now, as the daily maintain does, or with `--rebuild` build it again from nothing (see "Search index"). Optional: search scans whatever the index lacks. Prints a line for each project folder it indexed, merged or built again and a total, counts only, or with `--json` the index's status. A catch-up already running prints `index: busy` and exits 0; a failure exits 1 |
| `eva-md commit [--push-only]` | The daily commit and push now, with maintain's guards, or with `--push-only` a push of earlier commits. Prints one line, such as `committed (3 pages, 1 left out), push ok`; exits 1 when the commit was refused or failed, or a push-only run that ran didn't end `ok` or `up_to_date`; a run that found another one running (`busy`) exits 0 |
| `eva-md system write <slug> [--replace]` | Read one system page from stdin and write it with the map (see "System page"). Prints `wrote systems/<slug>.md (<n> bytes); the map has <m> systems`, then a line for each repo and commit of the code the page cites, saying the clone its paths were checked in or why they weren't; or each problem it refused the page for, by line, never the text. Exits 0 on a write, 1 on a refusal and 2 on a usage error |
| `eva-md system status [<slug>...] [--json]` | Measure how far the code the system pages cite has moved, or that of the pages named, in local clones, and save it as the code status the prompt hook and the doctor read (see "Code a system page cites"). Prints a line for each repo and commit, most moved first, one for each page past its review day, then the counts; `--json` prints the status. Exits 0 whatever the code did, 1 when the wiki can't be read or the status can't be saved, 2 on a usage error |
| `eva-md inbox clear [--session ID ...] [--project KEY] [--before YYYY-MM-DD] [--apply]` | Remove captured sessions whole, chosen by session, project and last write, each given narrowing the rest; a preview without `--apply`. Removes each session file with its watermark and, unless the chat is off the record, its recovery record, and the projects' search index, recording each session in `inbox/.removed` for the lint just before its files go; never the wiki (see "Removal" under "The inbox"). Exits 0 when done or when nothing matches, 1 when busy, on a failure or for 8 characters two sessions share, and 2 on a usage error, such as a filter given an empty value |
| `eva-md inbox list \| show <session>` | Planned: inspect the inbox |
| `eva-md migrate [--apply]` | Later: schema migration, previewed first (only once a breaking change exists) |
| `eva-md hook --host claude-code\|codex` | Hook entry point (internal) |
| `eva-md version` | The binary's version, then `schema_version 1`, the wiki schema it reads and writes. A release binary's version is its tag, such as `2026.11.3`, which the release workflow sets with `-ldflags '-X main.version=<tag>'`: the same day a source build of that commit prints (see "Making a release"). A source build's version is calendar based and set by no one: the UTC day of the commit the binary was built from, as year.month.day, then as build metadata that commit's UTC time as hours and minutes and its short hash, as in `2026.11.2+0915.9e4f609`, with `.dirty` for a build of a changed working tree. It is a valid semantic version; the time sorts two builds of one day, which compare equal under semantic versioning's rules, and the hash names each one. Go records the commit in every binary built from a git checkout, and in the pseudo-version of a module it fetched; a build with neither, such as a test binary, is `dev`. The same version is the author, `eva-md/<version>`, of the pages eva-md builds itself, and the MCP server's `serverInfo` version. |
| `eva-md mcp --host claude-code\|codex` | The read-only MCP server the plugins start once a chat (internal; see "MCP server") |

The built commands are setup, init, validate, install, uninstall, upgrade, doctor, status, brief, show, search, index, inbox, update, commit, maintain, system, hook, mcp and version; run with no arguments, eva-md lists them. The planned `inbox list` and `inbox show` print inbox's usage and exit 2; any other command, the planned `migrate` included, prints "unknown command" and exits 2.

---

## Reliability guarantees

| Guarantee | How |
| --- | --- |
| A hook never blocks or breaks a chat | Always exit 0, never 2, never a block decision. Panics recovered. Internal deadlines well under the host timeouts. No model or network in hooks. Slow work only in setsid-detached children with all stdio redirected. Codex Stop always gets `{}`. Claude Stop and async hooks print nothing. |
| Every finished exchange is saved | Documented payload fields, appended under flock with fsync. PostToolUseFailure for failed commands. StopFailure markers. Limited transcript catch-up. Gaps that can't be recovered are written as markers, never skipped silently. A config that exists but can't be read stops capture until it is fixed, and each chat start says so in one line. |
| "Where did we stop?" works even if no model ever runs | The brief comes from the raw inbox tail, plan entries and live git. The Now section is optional and flagged as stale when it is behind. The resume suite runs with the update switched off. |
| Every chat gets its brief once, within host caps | SessionStart with the first-prompt fallback, keyed by a per-session marker. Resume and fork inject only changes. Hard caps of 8,000 characters on Claude and 6,000 on Codex, whose hooks also set a 3000-token limit, with the footer and the newest exchange reserved first. |
| Compaction never drops the current state | PreCompact catches up and starts an update. SessionStart(compact) re-injects Now plus the last exchanges verbatim. |
| A failed or killed update loses nothing and rarely doubles anything | The watermark advances only after the output validates, and after the page's folder fsync when the page changed. Compare-and-swap. Unchanged saves are skipped. Leftover temp files are ignored and never staged. Catch-up at every chat start, and the day's sweep of idle backlogs with a person's first chat start of the day. A kill or a failed watermark write after a save can repeat Next items and done, change or problem lines on the next pass. |
| Pages never silently lose items or your text | Accountable item ids, an append-only Timeline, no-clobber create. A knowledge page keeps every statement it held in its History, and a retired page stays on the map. Locked, human-verified and damaged pages are respected. Your sections are kept and never rewritten. Out-of-order guard. |
| Dates and speakers come from the data | Refs to entries. Code-assigned dates. Any date in the text must appear in a cited entry. Tags are checked against the speakers of cited entries, and prompts you didn't type never count as yours. |
| Secrets stay out of git | Redaction at capture, on model output and on the staged diff. The inbox lives outside the wiki. Explicit-path staging that refuses `.jsonl`, inbox and `.tmp-okf-*` files. Quarantine for text you wrote. Codex updates that used a tool are rejected. |
| One wiki's memory never mixes with another's | One config per machine. A remote guard and an instance id checked on every write, commit and push. No API calls and no syncing between machines. |
| Commits never block a hook or nag | A detached daily job, one signing attempt per day, push retries with timeouts, never a force push. |
| A stuck or orphaned MCP server never holds the host or the hooks | The server writes nothing and takes no lock. Each call has a deadline; one still running a second past it, or a second past the host's cancel, is left running, and 8 such calls end the server. It exits when its stdin ends, a write fails or its parent process changes. |
| No recursion | `EVA_MD_INNER=1` makes the hook exit at once, and this holds even when managed-policy hooks run. Claude `--safe-mode`; Codex `--disable hooks --disable plugins`. An empty run folder and no session persistence. |
| A missing, stale or damaged search index never hides a file or shows text the inbox doesn't hold | The inbox is the truth and the index a cache of it. The scan covers what the index does not; every line the index proposes is read again from its file and checked before it is shown; the full scan runs when the index finds nothing; a damaged folder is scanned, and built again by the next catch-up. |
| Unknowns are stated as unknown | The brief says "unknown", "stale", "no reply recorded" or "no reply (stop_failure)". The doctor uses UNKNOWN for anything it can only infer, such as Codex trust. The author is recorded as `<host>/unknown` when the model is unknown. |

---

## Testing

The update's input builder, validator, renderer, runners and job, the offline accuracy suite with its rule and knowledge histories, the update's crash safety, system pages, the code they cite, knowledge pages, prompt pointers and recall, the brief's constraints, the daily commit and push and the setup states have their tests now (see [verification.md](verification.md)). The live accuracy runs and the live resume test with the update on are still to run.

- **Unit tests:**
  - remote normalization: 25 or more URL forms, worktrees, submodules, no remote, outside any repo
  - redaction corpus with positives and negatives (SHAs, UUIDs and `op://` must survive; fails closed)
  - fence calculation; caps that never split a character; watermark arithmetic; the escaper
  - branch-to-ID mapping: `index`, `.wip`, `-x`, deep names, unicode
  - the actor string always passes `IsValidActor`
- **Hook contract tests:**
  - recorded payloads for every event on both hosts, from the docs plus scrubbed live captures
  - exit 0 on garbage input
  - correct stdout shape: Codex Stop always `{}`; Claude Stop, async hooks and PostModelSwitch print nothing
  - tolerance of missing fields: Claude model absent; Codex `model` absent on SessionEnd, `turn_id` absent on SessionStart and SessionEnd, `transcript_path` and `last_assistant_message` null
  - subagent payloads (`agent_id`) never stored as user prompts
  - Codex `Exit code: N` parsing when supplied, empty-response `exit unknown` fallback, and apply_patch path parsing
  - the update triggers through an injected spawn: which events spawn for which sessions, at thresholds just below and at 60 KB, 30 minutes, 600 bytes and 2 minutes, the catch-up's limit of 5, a resumed chat's own backlog taken at its start and not again at its next prompt, nothing with `updates = false`, and nothing from a program-run Claude chat's own triggers, whose chat start still catches up the project's other idle sessions; and a real spawn from a hook process: the job in its own session, with no stdio of the hook's, outliving the hook, whose output ends at once
- **Inbox concurrency:** 8 writers with 1,000 appends each, and 4 writer processes, produce no interleaving. A line cut off mid-append, as a kill would leave it, reads as a gap marker, and the next append follows it without rewriting the file.
- **Deterministic resume suite** (temporary HOME, a repo with main plus 2 worktrees, scripted payloads):
  - startup, clear, resume, fork and compact, each with no page, a stale page and a fresh page
  - an Esc-interrupted last turn recovered by catch-up; a StopFailure marker
  - a chat with no real work skipped
  - lineage on a new branch
  - the first-prompt fallback for a spare session; the hash-triggered refresh, at most twice, after a compact brief too
  - a long-tenured Now (40 decisions) that keeps every next step and open question; the opening request from the head of a 5 MB session file
  - off-the-record content absent
  - Codex to Claude across hosts and back
  - hostile content (backtick runs, fake labels)
  - size caps
  - no assertion on Claude plan entries
- **okf adapter golden tests** (`generated.at` masked):
  - item accounting; strikethrough supersession; speaker downgrade
  - dates taken from refs, and unsourced dates rejected
  - idempotent re-apply leaves the file untouched
  - compare-and-swap retry; no-clobber create refuses an existing file
  - out-of-order guard; locked, human-verified and damaged pages
  - unknown sections and flat extra keys kept
  - fixed frontmatter order; `sources` as resource mappings
  - your `## Notes` with horizontal rules around a `Status:` line marks the page damaged, without a retry loop
  - okf Validate (Drift on) passes on the full layout with zero errors and zero orphans
  - index files sorted and unchanged when only thread content changes
  - a missing root index.md with a knowledge/ folder present is refused
  - SIGKILL during a save leaves a `.tmp-okf-*` file that is never staged, and okf's lock file, which the next save removes before its own
- **Accuracy suite, written fresh** (`internal/update`): 12 fact scripts, one per known failure mode (an assistant's suggestion against the user's decision, an event date against the date it was said, a reversal, a correction, a conditional fact, pasted text posing as a decision, a host prompt that must never become stated, item accounting across two passes, the two-session acceptance, the replace question and its exit, overlapping sessions processed in the wrong order, and an assistant's suggestion that survives as a proposal), and a 12-week tenure case. Each fact has a speaker, time, validity, the fact it replaces, a tag, a branch and a codename no model could infer. Code renders the scripts into inbox entries and derives the obligations: enforceable ones, which the validator guarantees whatever the model writes, checked after every output, and content ones, which depend on the model. Offline against prepared outputs in CI, where the correct outputs must score perfectly after every pass on the operation-level metrics (recall of planted facts, update accuracy, stale-value rate, speaker-tag and date accuracy, item accounting, duplicates). Live and opt-in per host, once updates run, with a target of every enforceable obligation met, zero speaker or date errors, and at most 2 content obligations missed. Seven knowledge histories run the same way, each pass saved into a real wiki: a fact superseded later, an assistant's suggestion, pasted text, a restatement, an older chat's supersede and drop, a locked page and a constraint with its path.
- **Recall precision** (`internal/knowledgepage`): 73 labelled prompts, each asked in its project, at indexes of 1, 3, 10, 36 and 500 rows, from the fixture's 36 rows of two projects and neutral rows that hold none of the prompts' words, with the two projects apart and sharing a system page, which fail the test below 90% precision or 60% recall at any size in either and log a sweep of floors at each size, the false positives and the misses.
- **Scope suite, written fresh (22 cases; planned):** one project across clones and worktrees; forks kept separate; folder identity; subfolders map to the top level; excludes; the wiki excludes itself; the general area; worktree chats resolved from payload `cwd`; one machine's instance guard refuses another machine's wiki.
- **Live accuracy run** (`cmd/live-accuracy`), opt-in, one host at a time: the accuracy suite's 12 fact scripts, which live in `internal/update/accuracy` so the offline suite and the run share them, run as the update job runs its passes. Each pass's batch is built for the page the passes before it made, sent once through the host's real headless update command with the update's rules and the page's schema, in an empty run folder, and the answer is checked by the validator and applied with `threadpage.Apply`; then every enforceable obligation is checked and the page measured. An answer the runner refuses as invalid or for a tool call, or the validator refuses, leaves the page and its `as_of` as they were, as a failed pass leaves them in the job, and counts as refused and as a content obligation missed. The page is still measured at that chat's time, so each fact the chat says and each value it ends counts as missed, as content whatever its kind, and the script's later passes, whose page is then older than they expect, are no longer held to its out-of-order guard. Any other host failure, such as auth, a rate limit or a timeout, stops the run unjudged, and so does an answer from the host's default model in place of a `--model` the host rejected, refused or not. It prints each history's metrics and misses, then the counts against the target: enforceable obligations missed, speaker errors (a fact under another tag), date errors (a Timeline line under another date) and content obligations missed (an answer refused, or a fact missing, not logged, still in force after it stopped holding, in two Now items, asserted though said off the record or pasted, or without its condition), each planted fact counted once a kind, and how many answers came at each effort the host used. It needs `--host` and refuses to run without `--spend-model-quota`, since it makes 21 model calls on the host's subscription, and refuses as a usage error an effort or model the runner would refuse and a `--cli` that names no executable file, which the runner would pass over for the CLI on PATH. It works in a temporary home of its own, which holds the run folders and is removed at the end, and reads and writes nothing of eva-md's; the host CLI keeps your usual login. It is a developer's command: no release or install step builds it, and eva-md never imports the suite.
- **Live resume test** (the headline), opt-in:
  - On each CLI, chat A plants a codename, a decision, an open question and a next step.
  - Then a new chat, /clear and forced compaction each ask "where did we stop?", and the answer must contain all four.
  - Run with the update off and on, and with chat B started while the update is still running.
  - Uses bounded synthetic fixture chats with the normal authenticated host and normally approved plugin hooks. State-only automated tests use an isolated HOME. Live tests never copy `auth.json`, manufacture trust records, or bypass hook trust. A fresh-home authentication route is a separate opt-in test, not a prerequisite for the normal installed route.
  - Both desktop apps are checked by hand for each release.
- **Git** (against a bare remote and a stub signer):
  - one commit per day; a declined signature not retried the same day; a pending push retried
  - a non-fast-forward recorded as `diverged`, never rebased or forced
  - remote and instance mismatch refused
  - inbox and `.tmp-okf-*` never staged
  - your signing config untouched
- **Packaging:**
  - `claude plugin validate --strict --json` on the marketplace root and the plugin folder
  - hooks JSON lint (integer timeouts, no code path exits 2)
  - **both plugin folders byte-identical across binary-only releases**
  - install, uninstall and rollback round trip under a temporary HOME
  - the install record, the link on PATH, and uninstall's preview, `--apply`, `--all` and the update's and the daily job's locks
  - the install and uninstall scripts and `eva-md upgrade` end to end against a release stand-in built in the test, served by a fake curl or a local test server, with the wiki and its bare remote unchanged by an upgrade, an uninstall and the install command run again on a Mac that is set up
- **Benchmark** (`scripts/benchmark.py`, run by hand before a change is finished, not a test, and by CI's macOS job as a comparison of the change's binary with the one before it, `--compare`, which reports and does not yet fail the job; see [verification.md](verification.md)): p95 under 50 ms for capture and under 300 ms for the brief on a 50 MB inbox, under 200 ms for SessionEnd with its spawn, under 50 ms for a prompt that recalls 3 facts from a recall index near its 512 KiB bound, and the MCP server's initialize, search, show and status figures. The tests themselves never depend on machine speed.
- **Releases** (`internal/release`): on macOS, `scripts/release.sh` run in a temporary repo with a bare origin, a fake gh and a fake signing program, on a new pseudo-terminal or none: each failed check with its fix, and in a workflow as an annotation, the tag's name, a dry run, a plan with no signing setup or terminal and what it writes for the workflow, a declined question, a published release and a wrong one, with no git environment from outside reaching it; on any system, every action in every workflow pinned to a commit, the same files summed, attested and published by the release workflow as the script and Cut a release check, Cut a release's run name, jobs, conditions, environment, permissions, time limits and one forceless tag push, no job in either workflow named with an expression, the tag job going on without a push only for origin's annotated tag on the planned commit with the planned notes, the release workflow's two triggers and the run's link in a called run's notes, one provenance signer everywhere with each way's source ref, and one tag pattern everywhere, agreeing with `ValidTag`; and `scripts/LICENSES.txt` holding the licenses of eva-md, of each module built into it and no other, and of Go, and each notice in Go's own code built into the binaries that asks to go with them (`cmd/eva-md`).
- **okf upgrade gate:** the golden corpus round trip, plus a re-save of a copy of the live wiki with a clean `git diff`.
- **Crash safety:** killing the update mid-run leaves the page and watermark unchanged, and the next run succeeds.
- **Search index:** the segment format read back exactly, across dictionary and location blocks; every single byte changed in a segment caught, and a fuzz test of the reader; refreshes, budgets, generations, merges equal to a fresh build, pruning and catch-up; nothing off the record or forged ever indexed; scores equal whether a line is scored from the index or from its text; every search test but the two cut short by a deadline run with and without the index, with the same hits; the fallback scan, tails, stale and damaged files, the top-up's thresholds and a fault on the index's path; 8 writers appending to shared session files with a refresh after each step; and 1,000 session files.
- **MCP server:** the protocol and its errors over pipes, the deadlines, cancellation and the abandoned-call limit, the off states, the fixed tool list, the instructions and their warnings, the three tools against a planted home, inbox and wiki, a real child process found by a test, and a hash of the home folder that 30 calls leave unchanged, with more unindexed chat in scope than `eva-md search` would index.

---

## v1 scope

### In

- One Go binary, eva-md (CLI, hooks, updater, maintain), plus okf v0.8.0 through a single adapter.
- Static plugins for Claude Code (CLI, desktop Code tab local and SSH) and Codex (CLI, ChatGPT app Codex mode), installed only through the hosts' plugin CLIs from local marketplaces.
- Releases on GitHub for Apple silicon and Intel Macs, cut with the Cut a release workflow or `scripts/release.sh`; `install.sh` and `eva-md upgrade` install or upgrade from them, and `eva-md uninstall` and `uninstall.sh` remove eva-md, never the wiki.
- Capture:
  - prompts and final replies
  - one-line tool records (edits, commands, reads, searches, web fetches and MCP calls, successes and failures; never output)
  - plan updates where the host provides them
  - Claude compaction summaries, model switches and API-error markers
  - redaction, off the record and the exclude list
  - desktop spare sessions deferred to the first prompt
  - subagent and non-typed prompts kept apart from your words
- Limited transcript catch-up for missing turns in known sessions, plus gap markers.
- Local JSONL inbox, 0600, never committed; `eva-md inbox clear` removes whole sessions from it, and without `--apply` previews what it would remove.
- Code-built brief for startup, clear, resume, fork and compact, plus the first-prompt fallback and the hash-triggered refresh, live git, freshness lines, fencing and per-host caps.
- `eva-md search` over the inbox, the thread pages, the system pages and the knowledge pages, run by the agent, with refs that `eva-md show` resolves.
- A rebuildable search index of the inbox, which the daily maintain and `eva-md index` keep and search reads beside its scan.
- A read-only MCP mode of the binary, `eva-md mcp`, with `eva_search`, `eva_show` and `eva_status`, which both plugins declare.
- Background update of the per-branch thread Now section, the standing rules and the knowledge pages by the chat's own host and model (headless, JSON schema), with code validation, accountable items, idempotent apply and catch-up.
- Wiki: init, thread pages, rules pages, a project now.md built by code, system pages with their map and names index, knowledge pages with their map and recall index, generated sorted index files, a log.md with a line for each page created or changed, and a schema file with the schema version, the instance id and when it was created.
- Pointers to system pages in the brief and on prompts that name a system, and a short memory reminder on prompts.
- Knowledge a prompt strongly matches, fenced, at most 3 facts a prompt; the project's constraints in startup and clear briefs; and the recall skill in both plugins.
- How far the code a system page cites has moved since it was written, measured in local clones: in the brief's and the prompts' pointers, in `eva-md show`, in `eva-md system status` and the daily job's code status, and in the doctor.
- Daily commit with one signing attempt and the push right after, with retries, remote and instance guards, and a secret scan.
- doctor (PASS, FAIL or UNKNOWN), a one-line start notice on Claude, problem notices on both hosts, and an opt-in live probe.
- Tests: the deterministic resume suite, the accuracy suite, hook contracts, redaction, okf adapter goldens, git and packaging; the scope suite and an automated live resume test are planned.

All of this is built except what is marked planned above. The live resume test with the update on, the live accuracy runs and the live checks listed in [verification.md](verification.md) are still to run, by hand.

### Out

- Claude Desktop Chat tab, regular ChatGPT chat, Claude Code cloud sessions, and the Codex IDE extension.
- Local models, vector databases, daemons or sleeping jobs, model calls on every turn, automatic deletion, history backfill, telemetry and auto-update.
- Writing host settings files, CLAUDE.md or AGENTS.md. GitHub or GitLab APIs, apart from setup running gh or glab to create or check the wiki's repo (so no open PRs). Running tests at chat start.
- Committing raw captures, per-chat transcripts or quotes of your words in pages.
- okf's registry, hub sync, vendor bundles, scope layers, governance tiers, scorecards, agent instruction block and MCP server, for knowledge pages too.
- Homebrew or another package manager, builds for Linux or Windows, and checking a release's build attestation in the scripts or `eva-md upgrade`.

## Roadmap

| Stage | What it covers | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Capture and resume** | The Go module with okf pinned; the hook dispatcher, redacted inbox, redactor and identity resolver; the okf adapter with all its protections; `eva-md init` and guided `eva-md setup`; both plugins installed through the hosts' own CLIs, with uninstall and rollback; capture of every entry kind, transcript recovery and off the record; all brief variants with the fallback and refresh, live git and freshness lines; heartbeats, the doctor and notices; `eva-md brief`, `eva-md show` and `eva-md search` over the inbox. | Built. What is checked is in [verification.md](verification.md). |
| **Wiki updates** | The updater for both hosts (including the Codex tool-use rejection), the validator, the renderer through the okf adapter, triggers and catch-up, project now.md, index files, the maintain job, the daily signed commit and push with all guards, and `doctor --live`. Done when the live resume test passes with the update on, the accuracy suite run live on each host meets every enforceable obligation with zero speaker or date errors and misses at most 2 content obligations, and a week of daily use needs no manual step except the daily signing approval. System pages, standing rules and pointers ship in this stage too. | Built and checked offline. The live checks in [verification.md](verification.md) are pending, so it is not done yet. |
| **Self-hosted git and managed machines** | Init of a wiki on a self-hosted GitLab, install, managed-settings detection, a proxy-aware live probe, and push retries when the server is out of reach. Done when the doctor is green or every FAIL or UNKNOWN is explained, and the instance guard is shown to refuse another machine's wiki. This closes v1. | Built and checked offline, with a fake ssh and an unreachable host. Setup has made a wiki on a self-hosted GitLab from a release, and the install command has installed eva-md there; the doctor, a day's commit and push and the instance guard on such a wiki are pending, so it is not done yet. |
| **Search index** | A rebuildable index of the inbox for `eva-md search`, with BM25 ranking within its tiers, the scan of what it lacks, the full scan when it finds nothing, the daily maintain and `eva-md index`. | Built and checked offline; its size and build time on a real inbox are still to be read from the doctor. |
| **MCP server** | `eva-md mcp`, a read-only MCP server over search, show and a project status, declared in both plugins, with its deadlines, caps, off switch and the doctor's rows. | Built and checked offline. The live checks in [verification.md](verification.md) are pending. |
| **Knowledge and recall** | Knowledge pages the update promotes, supersedes and drops, with their map and recall index; recall of strongly matching knowledge on prompts; the brief's constraints; search, show and `eva_status` over the pages; the doctor's row and repair, maintain's step; and the recall skill. Each piece ships with its own test, and recall with its own latency case in the benchmark, which must not slow the hooks or the resume. | Built and checked offline, at the user's request rather than after two weeks of daily v1 use (see "Changes to decisions"). The recall floor is tuned on fixtures; real use and the live checks in [verification.md](verification.md) are pending. |
| **Releases, install and upgrade** | GitHub releases cut with the Cut a release workflow or `scripts/release.sh` and built, checked, attested and published by the release workflow; `install.sh`, `uninstall.sh` and `eva-md upgrade`; the install record, the link on PATH and `eva-md uninstall --all`. | Built and checked offline: the release script against a temporary origin and a fake gh, the workflows by reading them, and the scripts and `eva-md upgrade` against a release stand-in. Releases cut from a Mac and, since 2026.11.5, from GitHub are published on GitHub; the install command has installed eva-md from one, and the uninstall script has run from one up to the removal of Claude Code's marketplace. `eva-md upgrade` and a whole uninstall against a real release are pending. |

## Changes to decisions

Where this design refines a decision in [decisions.md](decisions.md), and why.

| Decision | Change | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1. The wiki lives in a private repo | Private, or internal after you agree, when the host's admins forbid private repos or the repo you give is internal: setup tries private first, asks before it creates or uses an internal repo (no is the default), takes `EVA_MD_ALLOW_INTERNAL_REPO` as the answer in advance (`true` agrees to any internal repo, on any host), and refuses one without a terminal or a yes. A public repo is always refused. A bare repo on this Mac also holds a wiki. | A self-hosted GitLab can restrict private visibility to its admins, so a private repo there is impossible for anyone else; the user decided that an internal repo, readable by everyone signed in to that host, is acceptable once they agree to it. |
| 1. You create the empty repo; never GitHub or GitLab APIs | Only `eva-md setup` may run gh or glab, and only when it is installed and signed in: `auth status`, `config get` (the git protocol and glab's host), `repo view` and `repo create`, to create the wiki's private repo, or an internal one once the user agrees where the host's admins allow no private repo, to check that a repo is private, and to read back a repo's address, also to clone a missing wiki folder again. Reading a repo's schema before using it is plain git. The wiki, init's git steps, the hooks, the update job and the daily commit and push stay plain git. | Creating the repo was the one manual step left, and a public wiki repo is the costliest mistake setup can catch. The CLIs keep the user's own login, and nothing after setup depends on them. |
| 1. The platform uses only the plain git command line | `eva-md upgrade` and the install and uninstall scripts download eva-md's own releases from github.com over HTTPS (`releases/latest` and `releases/download/<tag>/<name>`), with no API call and no sign-in. Nothing else does, and hooks never do. | A one-command install and upgrade needs the release files, and public files over HTTPS need no token and no API. |
| 4. Hooks save every exchange word for word | An exchange is your prompt plus the final assistant message, plus one-line tool records (successes and failures), plan updates where the host has them, and Claude's compaction summary, except once the chat has been off the record, until a startup or clear chat start. Assistant narration between tool calls and all tool output are not saved. Prompts you didn't type are saved as events. | These are the documented payload fields (Claude's `last_assistant_message` is the final reply only). Transcript formats are not a stable interface, and tool output is where secrets and noise live. |
| 5. After each chat, a detached job runs the update | "After each chat" means any of: SessionEnd, PreCompact, a 60 KB backlog at Stop, the next prompt after a 30-minute gap, or catch-up at the next chat start, with the day's sweep of up to 5 backlogs idle an hour or more at a person's first chat start of the day. At most 40 model calls per host per day, at the configured effort. Codex's 30-minute SessionEnd counts only for chats closed in every client. | Desktop chats rarely end and long chats rarely compact. There is still no model call per turn and no sleeping process. |
| 5. ...with tools off | Claude: tools off (`--tools ""`). Codex: each tool feature on eva-md's list (Codex 0.166.0's) that `codex features list` names is disabled, and any run that still used a tool is rejected and retried later. | Codex ignores the opt-out for unified exec, and several tool features stay on unless disabled one by one. Rejecting tool use enforces the intent. |
| 5. Promotes lasting facts into wiki pages | Lasting facts are kept as dated, sourced Timeline lines on the branch's thread page, and the update promotes those that pass the six-month bar into knowledge pages, from the batch it reads: one statement a page, superseded or dropped with a History line, never deleted. Chats a pass has already covered are not read again for it. | Refs can name only the batch's entries, so only what the batch states can be checked, and a backfill over earlier chats is ruled out. |
| 6 and 16. Strong matches on a prompt are added quietly; knowledge pages come after day one | Knowledge pages, the recall skill and prompt recall were planned to start after two weeks of daily v1 use. They were built at the user's request on 2026-11-02, rather than after two weeks of daily v1 use: a prompt that strongly matches gets the fact itself (at most 3 facts in 1,000 characters, label and fence included, 900 until the label came to say what the tags rest on; weak matches add nothing), only the background update writes knowledge pages, and a new chat's brief lists the project's constraint pages only (within 600 characters). okf's team-sharing features (registry, vendor bundles, hub sync, scope layers, scorecards) stay out. | The user chose to have them now. Each part is checked offline against fixtures, recall reads one local file, and a second small one only to tell which projects share a system page, within the prompt hook's latency target, and `recall = false` turns it off; the floor and the promotion bar are still to be judged in real use. |
| 5 and 6. After each chat, a job runs the update; a new chat resumes the previous one | A Claude chat a program runs (`claude -p`, the Agent SDK, told by `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`) is captured, but asks for no update of its own, is passed over by the update as `skipped (headless)` with no model call, and is never the brief's previous chat or lineage pick. | A program's prompts are stored as events, not as your words, so a thread page or a resume built from them would rest on no prompt you typed. |
| 6 and 16. The agent searches further on its own; search comes after day one | Deterministic, agent-initiated search over the inbox, the thread pages, the system pages and the knowledge pages (`eva-md search`) is part of v1. On prompts, v1 adds pointers to the system pages a prompt names, matched by name with no model, the knowledge a prompt strongly matches, scored by words with no model, and a short reminder of the two commands. | When the agent ran follow-up searches it found everything 10 times out of 10, and the inbox already holds every exchange word for word. A scan needs no model, index or MCP server, and it never runs in a hook. |
| 6 and 16. Search: the scan | Once history spans years, the inbox gets a rebuildable search index, a cache built from it that search reads beside the scan, with the scan as the fallback whenever the index finds nothing. Matching in indexed files is by whole tokens and word starts, and equally good hits rank by BM25 before newest. | A scan costs 1.7 to 2.9 s per GB for a rare word, and a common one is cut at the 5-second limit, more each year. Every line the index proposes is read again from the inbox, so it never changes the text of a hit; in indexed files a match inside a word, or in text without spaces, goes unseen next to other hits. |
| 7. One commit a day, one signing approval | The day's commit starts at the first hook event of the day, not only at a chat start. One signing attempt per day. A refusal is not retried until tomorrow or a manual `eva-md commit`. The SSH push may need a second approval, depending on your signing and SSH agent setup (not yet counted; see Open host questions). | A missed prompt must never turn into a stream of prompts. Signing is never changed. |
| 9. One binary also serves as the MCP server | It does: `eva-md mcp` is a read-only MCP server over `eva-md search`, `eva-md show` and a project status, which the plugins start once a chat. It writes nothing, and the brief's footer and the prompt reminder still name the command line. | Nothing in the resume path needs MCP, so the server only adds tools, and the command line works where a host didn't connect it. |
| 11. Each project has a Now page with a dated timeline | The Now and Timeline content lives in per-branch thread pages. The project's now.md is an overview of its threads, built by code. | Decision 16 chose one thread per branch plus a short overview. |
| 11 and 15. okf owns the page format; the generated index; append-only logs | okf owns page parsing, serializing, single-file writes and validation. eva-md writes every file of the scaffold but its pages, the root index.md included; `okf.InitBundle` is still called and writes nothing. eva-md also writes **log.md** (local dates, oldest first, union merge; a line for each page saved, once a day) and the **index.md files** (sorted, in okf's exact line format) itself. `AppendLogEntry`, `UpdateParentIndex` and `RelateConcepts` are never called. Both stay in eva-md until an okf release covers them. | okf's log is newest-first with UTC dates and no append mode. Its index appends unsorted, never prunes, and can't be rebuilt after a rebase. `RelateConcepts` always logs. |
| 12. The next chat catches up on anything a hook missed | Catch-up fills missing turns only for sessions that fired at least one hook, so the transcript path is known. Chats where no hook fired are reported, not swept from host stores. | Sweeping host stores amounts to the ruled-out history backfill, on unstable formats. |
| 12. Codex needs re-approval after each update | Keep the Codex plugin folder byte-identical across binary-only releases and call a binary at a stable path. The doctor and a needs-trust notice still catch a lapse. | On Codex 0.164.0, trust and capture survive a binary-only replacement and an identical reinstall. A description-only manifest change marks all seven hooks as modified until the exact folder is restored. |
| 12. Accuracy and scope suites | Both are written fresh: an accuracy suite of 12 fact scripts, one per known failure mode, whose obligations code derives, which is built, and a scope suite, which is planned. | Fact scripts let code derive every obligation and check the enforceable ones after every output. |
| 13 and 15. Its own names; okf at the core | eva-md shows its own name only. okf is named nowhere you see: not in `eva-md version`, the doctor, a message, an error or the run log, and not in a new wiki's files. A save that fails on okf's temporary file or its lock file says the step that failed in eva-md's words. The root index is eva-md's own, written before `okf.InitBundle` runs, and has no `okf_version` line, which okf's spec makes optional; `.gitignore` covers okf's temporary files with `.tmp-*`; `.eva-md/schema.toml` records no okf version, so the doctor compares none with the binary's. okf still names its own temporary files `.tmp-okf-*` and its lock file `.okf-write.lock`, which eva-md can't change without patching it, each release's `LICENSES.txt` carries okf's license, which asks to go with every copy, and these docs credit it. | eva.md is the front-facing name, and the name of a library under it reads as another product's message or as a fault. Every okf upgrade is checked before it ships (see "Pin and upgrade policy"), while a wiki's recorded okf version was never updated, so after an upgrade the doctor would have failed on every Mac. |
| 15. Protections the adapter adds | The list is no longer "no overwrite on create, one writer, supersede, real host as author". It becomes: no-clobber create, one wiki lock, compare-and-swap, supersede, actor validated before writing, skipping unchanged saves, folder fsync, a body pre-check, guards around LoadBundle, and eva-md-owned log and index files. | okf already records the author through `SaveOptions.Actor` (mutate.go:489-511 in v0.8.0). The real gaps are no validation on write, a fixed clock, no folder fsync, and the behaviours listed in the adapter section. |
| 15. okf owns search | eva-md's search does not use okf's `SearchAdvanced`; it reads its own rebuildable index of the inbox and scans what the index lacks and the thread, system and knowledge pages itself, one file at a time. Prompt recall reads eva-md's own recall index. `SearchLayered` is never used. | `SearchAdvanced` reloads and re-tokenizes the whole wiki for every query. `SearchLayered` depends on the working directory, the home folder and environment variables. |
| 16. Tool activity: files changed and commands run | Edits and commands get one line each, and so do reads, Grep and Glob searches, web fetches and MCP calls, by what they acted on (a path, a pattern, a URL, the arguments), never their output; a web search on Claude Code gets a line with the tool's name alone, never its query or results, so the update can tell a turn that read the web; other tools, such as a subagent, get none (see the plugins' tool matchers). Arguments and URL parts whose names say they carry a credential are left out. | What a chat read, such as a page of documentation or a file, is what a later chat in another repo asks about, so recall needs it, not only what changed. |
| 16. Managed machines: the health check reports managed settings that switch hooks off | Managed settings are not read. The doctor judges a policy by hook heartbeats, and on Codex by the hooks feature that `codex features list` reports; Claude's policy row is UNKNOWN. | Settings-file guesses can't see every managed source, and heartbeats show what actually fires. |
| 16. Visibility: one line at the start of a chat | The routine start line appears on Claude only. Codex stays silent when healthy and shows a line only when something is wrong. | Codex displays systemMessage as a warning. |

---

## Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- |
| Codex hook trust lapses after an update, and capture stops silently. Trust covers more than the command: a manifest-only change invalidated it in live CLI checks. A tool that rewrites `~/.codex/config.toml` (where trust is stored) can also reset it. | Byte-identical plugin folder; a needs-trust flag shown in the next Claude brief; heartbeats compared with Codex activity; the doctor; Codex's own startup warning. How the desktop app shows the trust prompt is still an open host question. |
| A Codex update run can still use tools (unified exec can't be disabled), and the read-only sandbox can read the whole disk, so injected text could pull local secrets into a page. | Disable each tool feature on eva-md's list that `codex features list` names; reject any run whose event stream shows a tool; refs must point to batch entries; redaction on output; optionally ignore user config. |
| Prompts you didn't type (scheduled tasks, /loop, subagent reports, cross-session messages) get stored as your words and promoted as "stated". | Detect them by payload field or wrapper shape; tag `agent_id` payloads; tell a program-run Claude chat by its entrypoint, store its prompts as host prompts, and have the update pass over it with no model call and the brief never take it as the previous chat, which the doctor shows per event; later, have the doctor count suspicious prompts. No reliable signal for scheduled tasks, /loop, subagent reports or cross-session messages on Claude has been observed yet (see Open host questions); if none exists, accept the risk: these sources are rare in normal use. |
| Desktop spare sessions, untrusted folders or a failed SessionStart deliver no brief. | The first-prompt fallback, keyed by a marker file; to be checked by hand in both desktop apps. |
| On current Claude models the plan tools are off by default, so the "open plan" part of the Claude brief is usually empty. | The brief and tests never rely on plan entries; Now items and raw exchanges carry the next steps. |
| The local Claude marketplace loads in place, so a half-written or deleted folder breaks hooks in every new Claude session at once. | Write only on change, swap with renames, never delete while installed, uninstall in order; the doctor checks `readFromFolder` and `errors`. |
| Background updates use your subscription quota on large models, more so at the default highest effort. | Effort configurable per host and per model; 60 KB input cap; triggers only at chat boundaries and thresholds; tiny backlogs skipped; 40 model calls per host per day; `updates = false` stops them; budget shown by the doctor. |
| Headless flags or authentication change in a host release, or the subscription login can't be read from a detached job. | Only the update degrades and the resume is unaffected; `doctor --live` catches it; per-machine flag overrides are planned; a notice after 3 failures. |
| `--tools ""` removes the tool `--json-schema` relies on, so every Claude update returns nothing. | It works on Claude 2.1.311; `doctor --live` will recheck it; fallback to JSON in the text result, validated in code. |
| Managed-policy hooks on a managed machine still run inside the Claude update job despite `--safe-mode`. | `EVA_MD_INNER=1` is checked first in every eva-md hook; the doctor reports what it can see of managed policy. |
| Model output drifts, misattributes speakers or drops open items. | Strict schemas checked by the host and again in code; speaker and date checks; accountable item ids; the watermark is held on failure; the accuracy suite as a release gate. |
| okf behaviour shifts on upgrade: input rules got stricter in 5 releases, one patch release broke the API, and the serializer or index format could change. | Pin (v0.8.0); upgrade only on need, on a branch, behind golden and live-wiki round-trip tests; anything okf lacks is covered in eva-md. |
| okf's direction drifts toward its hub, vault and registry, with one maintainer writing about 88% of commits. | eva-md uses only a small core; the adapter is the only coupling; the wiki stays plain markdown, so the core can be swapped. |
| A power loss after the watermark advances loses a page update. | fsync the folder before advancing the watermark. |
| One stray symlink, unreadable .md or frontmatter-less .md in the wiki fails LoadBundle or Validate for the whole wiki. | The doctor names the file; Validate runs only in the doctor and the commit, never in hooks or the resume. |
| okf's body check makes a page with your own horizontal-rule sections impossible to save. | The page becomes read-only (damaged) with a notice explaining the fix; no retry loop. |
| okf raises its Go version again, forcing a newer toolchain at upgrade time. | Accept it at upgrade time; the toolchain is local. |
| Go 1.27's encoding/json (backed by v2) changes edge cases in okf's parser. | `go test ./pkg/okf` and the adapter goldens run under the Go 1.27 toolchain. |
| Redaction misses a new secret format. | The inbox is stored only on this Mac (0600) and never committed, though the background update sends each chat's new entries to that chat's host CLI and its model; pages are short summaries; redaction runs again on the update's output and on the staged diff; quarantine; private repos. |
| Stored prompt injection persists through memory. | Fence and factual label; escaping; no-tool updates; assistant text never shown as yours; decisions you didn't make demoted to proposals; no knowledge observed from a turn that read the web, as far as its pass can see; recall of another project's knowledge only when it shares a system page or the prompt names it. |
| Several signing prompts in a day (for example from a password manager). | One signing attempt per day; the push runs right after in the same process; the approval count is still to be measured (see Open host questions); signing never disabled. |
| Transcript formats drift and break catch-up. | Capture never depends on transcripts; catch-up fails soft with fixtures; gaps reported as markers; three turns in a row with no recognized row record drift, which the doctor and a notice report. |
| Codex PostToolUse for long unified-exec commands arrives late or is cancelled at session end, so the last tool line can be missing. | Tool lines are a convenience; the brief marks missing exit codes as unknown. |
| Your MCP servers start on every Codex update run (slow; a required one that fails aborts the run). This is accepted by default (decision 17). | The `mcp_failed` reason and a notice; `codex_ignore_user_config` can switch it off for one machine. |
| Codex loading a plugin's `.mcp.json` through the manifest's `mcpServers` field is inferred from OpenAI's own plugins, not observed with eva-md, and the folder and environment Codex gives the server are not documented. | Hooks and the skill load either way; only the tools would be missing, which the live check catches. The server takes its home folder from the binary's path without HOME, looks for git in the system's folders without PATH, and says when it fell back to the general area, so the agent passes `cwd`. |
| Claude Code asks permission for an MCP tool unless a rule allows it. | eva-md never writes host settings; the README says how to allow `mcp__plugin_eva-md_eva-md__*` in your own Claude settings. |
| Codex shows no sign of using server instructions, so a hook problem may not reach the agent there. | `eva_status` says it; the Claude brief's notice and the doctor report it as before. |
| One idle MCP server per open chat. | No idle work but a parent check every 5 s, a 64 MiB soft memory limit, and an exit with its chat. |
| Threads keyed by branch pile up after merges. | Nothing is deleted. Working out idle, merged and gone threads from git, and folding idle ones in the overview, are planned. |
| Scope creep. | A written v1 scope; every stage gated on "installed and used daily". Knowledge pages and recall were built before two weeks of use, at the user's request, so the promotion bar and the recall floor are judged on fixtures until real use shows them. |
| Large monorepos make git slow at chat start. | `--no-optional-locks`; an 800 ms deadline; "unknown" on timeout; no fetch. |

---

## Ideas borrowed from ai-memory

akitaonrails/ai-memory (v3.2.8, MIT) was evaluated both on its own and as the core under eva-md, and not chosen as an engine because of fit:
- It needs a server that is in practice always running. Hooks can't reliably stop it.
- It keeps sessions, handoffs and history in SQLite, which the markdown can't rebuild.
- It commits unsigned on every event, with no switch to turn that off, in an ID-named layout. Your repo would hold only a copy.
- By default it downloads a local model, imports past sessions and deletes pages. These can be turned off.
- It can't be pinned safely: security fixes go only to the latest release, and migrations are forward-only.

These patterns are still worth taking into eva-md's own code:

| Pattern | Where it applies | Source in ai-memory |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Skip Codex's own memory-writing runs | Capture guards. Done | `crates/ai-memory-core/src/scaffolding.rs:104-136` |
| Ask Codex for hook trust through `codex app-server` hooks/list | Doctor. Done | `crates/ai-memory-cli/src/commands/codex_hook_trust.rs` |
| Keep the hook command identical across releases | Codex packaging. Done: the whole plugin folder stays byte-identical | `plugins/claude-code/scripts/ai-memory-hook` |
| Transcript filter rules: Claude meta rows, task notifications and compaction markers; Codex environment, permissions and AGENTS.md text inside user turns | Catch-up reader. Done | `crates/ai-memory-workstream/src/transcript.rs` |
| One shared fixture table for remote-URL identity | Identity resolver tests | `crates/ai-memory-core/fixtures/remote_identity_cases.json` |
| Redact before truncating; match across zero-width characters; strip ANSI, bidi and NUL characters | Redactor. Done | `crates/ai-memory-core/src/sanitize.rs` |
| When picking the previous chat, skip prompts that are only acknowledgments and flag a trailing unanswered question | Brief builder | `crates/ai-memory-hooks/src/router.rs:5145-5287` |
| Call a host CLI safely: cleared environment with an allowlist, a private temp folder, capped output and a timeout | Background update. Done | `crates/ai-memory-llm/src/codex.rs`, `cursor_agent.rs` |
| A CI check that released schema migrations never change | Schema versioning | `scripts/check-migrations-immutable.sh` |

Its own benchmark also supports eva-md's choice of search without a model. Plain full-text search beat its default local embedder: hit@1 was 0.857 against 0.766, and the median query took 11 ms against 157 ms.

---

## Open host questions

Host behaviour not yet observed. Each is an assumption in this design until it is observed.

**Claude Code**
- Folder trust: in a never-trusted folder, whether SessionStart fires before trust is accepted. Either way, the first prompt after trust should deliver the brief.
- Prompts you didn't type: whether a reliable field or wrapper shape tells /loop iterations, scheduled tasks, background subagent reports and cross-session messages apart from typed prompts. If none exists, the risk stays as documented.
- `prompt_id` stability: one stable id per typed prompt across UserPromptSubmit, PostToolUse and Stop, in a turn that includes a background subagent report.
- Desktop spare sessions: whether SessionStart fires again with the real `cwd` when a pre-started session is claimed, and whether the first UserPromptSubmit carries it and delivers the brief.
- Entrypoints: which `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` value each surface sets (the CLI, the desktop app, the editor extension), that print mode is `sdk-cli` and the Agent SDK `sdk-ts` or `sdk-py`, and that the desktop app and the editor extension set their own value, never an SDK one, which would make each of their chats pass as a program's, with no update of its own and never the brief's previous chat. One chat per surface, then the doctor's per-event rows, confirms it.
- Pasted text: whether hook payloads carry Claude Code's paste wrapper with matching ids. If the host strips it, the Claude side of the paste rule does nothing.
- The digest and recall skills: that `claude plugin validate --strict` accepts the plugin with them, and that each triggers.
- A deleted marketplace folder: what `claude plugin list --json` reports for eva-md once `~/.local/share/eva-md` is gone, and whether its uninstall and marketplace remove succeed then.
- The MCP server: that `claude plugin validate --strict` accepts `.mcp.json`, that `/mcp` shows eva-md connected with three tools, whether the first call of each tool asks permission, whether the tools are deferred behind ToolSearch in a session with few servers, and that quitting the app leaves no `eva-md mcp` process.
- Payload samples still missing: PostToolUseFailure (a failing Bash, whose error should start with "Exit code N"), StopFailure, PostCompact, PostModelSwitch, SessionStart with and without a model, and PostToolUse for TaskCreate, TaskUpdate and TodoWrite.

**Codex**
- Trust across surfaces: whether hooks trusted in the CLI fire in the ChatGPT app's Codex mode without a second review, and whether the app needs a restart to load the plugin.
- `--ignore-user-config` on a managed machine: auth from `CODEX_HOME`, no user MCP servers, and whether the configured provider still applies.
- Programmatic prompts in the ChatGPT app: whether UserPromptSubmit fires for tasks and automation messages, and whether a field marks them as not typed.
- Codex's own memory-writing runs: eva-md's hooks either don't fire for them or the capture guard skips them, and nothing from them reaches the inbox.
- `codex exec`: whether it fires hooks, and whether the transcript's `session_meta.payload.source` exists before the first prompt's hook runs, which would let its prompts be stored as host prompts. Codex has no known paste marker.
- The digest and recall skills: whether Codex loads them through the manifest's `skills` field, and whether each triggers.
- Plugin add and remove signed in to ChatGPT: whether `codex plugin add`, `codex plugin remove` and the marketplace's add and remove ask chatgpt.com too, and, on a network that holds that request, wait longer than the 30 seconds its list of every plugin waits.
- The MCP server: whether Codex loads the plugin's `.mcp.json` through the manifest's `mcpServers` field, the folder and environment it gives the server, whether `--disable plugins` keeps plugin servers out of `codex exec` update runs, whether it asks to approve the server or its first call, whether it shows server instructions, and the tool names its PostToolUse payloads carry.

**Both hosts and git (for wiki updates)**
- Detached jobs: a 60-second setsid job started from a hook finishes after quitting the Claude desktop app and, separately, the ChatGPT app.
- Signing approvals (for example from a password manager) for the daily commit: the number of approvals for a signed commit plus an SSH push from a detached job (target 1, accept 2), with no retry after a decline.
